# ADJUDICATION PASS (§F2) -- `m88kexcdeliver` (#502, HIGH): the panel SHARES its pass-2 findings.  Tree `GXEMUL-SEC/src/`, HEAD `83df95c` + the UNCOMMITTED round (diff to cpu_m88k.c 63+/12-: the seven-class delivery fix, comments corrected per pass 2; new `regress/m88k_exc_probe.py` v3 (22 fault rows + 2 controls); gate 11 wiring).  2026-09-05, ~23:05.

## CONTEXT AND VOCABULARY
An ordinary correctness review of an open-source machine emulator's CPU model (GXemul 0.7.0, hardened fork): the MC88100
exception-delivery path in `cpu_m88k.c`.  The "guest" is the operating system inside the emulator.  Plain engineering vocabulary
("the emulation stops", "the exception is delivered").  Read the project's own records NARROWLY (grep anchors, a few lines of
context; never whole CHANGELOG blocks or the ledger) and paraphrase in neutral terms.

## READ THIS FIRST -- PLEASE DO NOT SIMPLY RATIFY
A recent panel had seven of nine seats confirm a premise this brief's author stated wrongly.  **Check the premises, not just the
derivations.**  THE TREE UNDER REVIEW IS `GXEMUL-SEC/src/`; three pristine-2021-upstream copies sit under the project root and are
NOT this fork.  **If you cannot read files that is expected** -- every fact you need is inlined; name any premise you cannot settle
rather than guessing.  ENUMERATE arithmetic rather than reasoning about it.  **VERDICT LINE FIRST.**  Cite by grep anchor.

**THIS IS A THIRD PASS, not another review.**  Until 2026-08-30 every seat answered blind to the other eight and the main loop
arbitrated alone.  Below is every seat's pass-2 finding, numbered, WITH THE MAIN LOOP'S DISPOSITION and what changed as a result;
the disagreements separately; and what is still owed.  **Three questions**: (1) adjudicate F1..F24 and D1..D3 -- UPHOLD / OVERTURN
/ REFINE / CANNOT SETTLE, one line each with a reason; (2) WHICH OF MY ACCEPTED DISPOSITIONS IS WRONG?  I accepted 21 of 24
findings and repaired the detector on 13 of them in one evening -- that is a suspicious number and agreement is not evidence;
(3) WHAT DID ALL NINE OF US MISS?  A seat re-reading its OWN finding is doing self-review: be HARDER on it, and check that the
REPAIR is right, not only that the finding was.  This pass is also the REPAIR's pass 2 (the v3 probe is new floor-bound material):
the chain closes on the first VALID pass finding zero floor breaches -- so state your denominator: which cells / mutant shapes you
swept and found closed.

## 1. SETTLED PREMISES (re-inlined; each a QUESTION if you can check it)
* The fix: seven trap-like classes (MISALIGNED 4, UNIMPLEMENTED 5, PRIVILEGE 6, BOUNDS 7, ILLEGAL_DIVIDE 8, OVERFLOW 9, ERROR 10)
  gain `implemented = true` at VERBOSITY_INFO in switch 1 of `m88k_exception()` AND bare `case` labels in the tail switch; the
  fallback (`vector < 128 && !implemented` -> stop every CPU) and the tail `default:` are untouched.  Product lines unchanged
  since 16:16; ONLY the two comments changed at 23:00 (F19-F22 below).
* Two routes into switch 1: INSTRUCTION faults (is_trap 0) and supervisor `tb0/tb1/tcnd` naming any vector (is_trap 1; the
  user-mode filter in X(tb0) raises PRIVILEGE with is_trap 0).  The SFRZ re-route (`if (SFRZ) { if (!is_trap) vector = ERROR; ...
  update_shadow_regs = false; }`) runs AFTER switch 1.  BOUNDS (7) has no instruction raise site (`tbnd` undecoded, row m88ktbnd);
  ERROR's switch-1 arm is reached only by a trap naming 10.
* MEASURED, all logs in `regress/artifacts/m88kexcdeliver/` (untracked until the commit): HEAD 0/9 (probe2_head.out), 0/10
  (probe10_head.out); the fix 9/9 -> 10/10 -> 22/22 (probe_v3_fix.out, 21:52).  TABLE 1 (13 mutants, 9 rows): 12 killed, M_LEVEL
  survived (then thought unobservable), M_ERRORDROP ESCAPED 9/9.  TABLE 2 (errortb added): M_ERRORDROP 9/10, M_ERRORTAIL 8/10,
  M_REVERT1 9/10.  OPUS SEAT (pass 2, 15 mutants, 10 rows): M_NOVBR, M_SFRZTRAP, M_SFIPNOTGT, M_DSLOTSWAP, M_DELAYALWAYS, M_TAILOFF
  all 10/10 ESCAPED; M_ISTRAPBOUNDS 10/10 EQUIVALENT; M_MODECOND 3/10, M_VEC7 5/10, M_EPSRAFTER 0/10, M_IMPLOVF 9/10 killed.
  TABLE 3 (v3 probe, 22 rows, frozen md5 28302f55, 21:54-22:04, clone clean before and after, BASE 22/22 both ends):

      M_ISTRAP      17/22  killed: misaligntb unimpltb div0tb ovftb privsuptb (HALT)
      M_NOVBR       21/22  killed: vbrtb (STOP -- pc 0x38, entry 0x2038)
      M_SFRZTRAP    21/22  killed: trapfrozen (STOP -- re-routed to 0x50)
      M_SFIPNOTGT   21/22  killed: dslot bad=sfip          M_DSLOTSWAP  21/22  killed: dslot bad=snip,sfip
      M_CARRYCLR    21/22  killed: ovfc bad=psr            M_LEVEL       3/22  killed: 19 rows bad=level
      M_DELIVERALL  20/22  killed: resid50 TAILHALT, sfu1h STOP (the negative controls)
      M_TAILLE      21/22  killed: usertb TAILHALT         M_TRAPONLY   22/22  EQUIVALENT (disclosed: no instruction raises 7)
      M_REVERT       3/22  M_ERRORDROP 21/22 (errortb)     M_DSLOT 21/22 (dslot)     M_NAIVE 3/22
  (3/22 = the two HALT-expected controls + usertb, which the fallback never touched.)
* The v3 rows: div0 ovf misalign bounds priv privtb unimpl error dslot errortb | misaligntb unimpltb div0tb ovftb privsuptb (trap
  routes) | vbrtb (VBR=0x2000, entry 0x2038) | trapfrozen (div0 handler = tb1 naming 7 with SFRZ set -> 0x38, shadows frozen) |
  ovfc (PSR C set before the overflowing add -> 0x9000000b) | udiv0 (user-mode divu by zero -> vector 8) | usertb (tb1 naming 128
  -> 0x400) | resid50, sfu1h (EXPECT HALT) | live, attrib.  dslot now uses br.n +16 (SNIP 0x1000e, SFIP 0x10016 -- two DIFFERENT
  numbers).  Every DELIVER row also asserts no `exception <CLASS>` line in the free-running transcript (the LEVEL channel).  The
  probe exits non-zero on any failure.  Gate 11 pins `M88KEXC_RESULT=22/22`, the two controls, resid50/sfu1h HALT, vbrtb at 0x2038,
  trapfrozen at 0x38, ovfc PSR 0x9000000b, and no bad=level.

## 2. THE FINDINGS, NUMBERED, WITH DISPOSITIONS
F1 (grok, deepseek, glm-mini -- independent) `if (!is_trap) implemented = true;` for classes 4/5/8/9 escapes the 10-row probe.
   ACCEPTED -> four trap-route rows misaligntb/unimpltb/div0tb/ovftb; M_ISTRAP killed 17/22.
F2 (codex, glm-mini) the same for PRIVILEGE: privtb arrives with is_trap 0 (the user-mode filter), so `implemented = !is_trap` on
   the PRIVILEGE arm leaves priv AND privtb green.  ACCEPTED -> row privsuptb (supervisor tb1 naming 6); M_ISTRAP extended to 5
   classes, killed.
F3 (Opus, MEASURED 10/10; kimi, Fable independently) `cpu->pc = 8 * vector` (VBR dropped) invisible: no row sets VBR.  L2 + L4
   (comment and contract claimed VBR).  ACCEPTED -> row vbrtb (stcr r5,cr7 = 0x2000 then tb1 naming 7; entry 0x2038); M_NOVBR
   killed 21/22; contract split into "wrong scaling (M_VEC4/M_VEC7)" and "dropped base (vbrtb)".
F4 (Opus, MEASURED 10/10; Fable, kimi) `if (!is_trap)` -> `if (true)` in the SFRZ re-route: the (SFRZ set, is_trap 1) cell empty;
   manual :8633-8636 / 6.10 :10613 / :9290-9294 make it load-bearing (a syscall from a frozen handler would become ERROR).  L2.
   ACCEPTED -> row trapfrozen; M_SFRZTRAP killed 21/22.
F5 (Opus, MEASURED 10/10 twice; codex, kimi) dslot's SNIP and SFIP expectations were ONE number (0x1000e): the SFIP channel
   vacuous by construction (L3).  ACCEPTED -> br.n +16 (SFIP 0x10016 != SNIP 0x1000e; an assert in the probe refuses a
   collapsed geometry); M_SFIPNOTGT and M_DSLOTSWAP killed.
F6 (Opus, codex, kimi, Fable -- four seats) F14 of pass 1 was WRONG: the exact PSR compare pinned carry only at its pre-fault
   value 0, never "unchanged".  ACCEPTED -> row ovfc (stcr r5,cr1 = 0x90000002 before the overflowing add; expect 0x9000000b);
   M_CARRYCLR (`(PSR & ~M88K_PSR_C) | ...`) killed 21/22.
F7 (Fable) "no row can observe the level" is FALSE: main.c lowers every subsystem to WARNING after startup, so an ERROR-level
   `[ exception <CLASS>: ]` line prints in the free-running transcript and INFO does not.  ACCEPTED -> the LEVEL channel on every
   DELIVER row; M_LEVEL killed 3/22 (19 rows bad=level).  The pass-1/2 "disclosed as unobservable" record is CORRECTED, not kept.
F8 (codex) tail guard `<` -> `<=`: vector 128 enters the tail default and stops; no row used an already-delivered vector.
   ACCEPTED -> row usertb (tb1 naming 128 -> 0x400); M_TAILLE killed 21/22.
F9 (kimi) NO NEGATIVE-CONTROL rows: the probe could not tell the shipped D1 form from the rejected D4 (tail default delivers) or
   from delivering SFU1.  ACCEPTED -> rows resid50 (tb1 naming 50: EXPECT HALT) and sfu1h (div0 handler = a legal divu with SFD1
   set -> SFU1 -> EXPECT HALT); M_DELIVERALL (fallback disabled) killed 20/22 on exactly those two.
F10 (Fable 9b, codex) "one user-mode divu by zero ended the emulation" was DERIVED, not measured (div0 is supervisor).
   ACCEPTED -> row udiv0 (stcr user mode, divu by zero -> vector 8 not SFU1); comment now cites div0/udiv0.
F11 (Opus) M_DELAYALWAYS (post-delivery `cpu->delay_slot` else-branch unpinned) 10/10 -- clause-(e) debt, needs a second
   UNFROZEN exception (a real new session).  ACCEPTED as DISCLOSED in the contract; FILED (m88kexcdebt).
F12 (Opus) M_TAILOFF (tail switch gated `< MISALIGNED`) 10/10 -- no rows for vectors 0..3 whose tail arms do real work.  Fable:
   equivalent BY CONSTRUCTION for the seven (bare breaks); Opus: escape because vectors 0..3 unrowed.  ACCEPTED as disclosed +
   FILED (m88kexcdebt: rows for vectors 0..3 = a different round: the hardware-event arms).
F13 (Opus, Fable, kimi) M_ISTRAPBOUNDS / M_TRAPONLY (`implemented = is_trap` at BOUNDS) EQUIVALENT today: no instruction raises 7.
   ACCEPTED -> measured 22/22 in table 3 and DISCLOSED with its warrant (m88ktbnd re-runs it).
F14 (Fable finding 1; codex; grok) THE DELAY-SLOT ELSE-ARM IS ARCHITECTURALLY WRONG: manual pointer definitions :2097-2104,
   :4207-4208, :8898-8902, Table 6-3 -- for a fault in the slot of a taken br.n, NIP = target, FIP = target+4; the fork sets
   SNIP = pc+4, SFIP = delay_target ("Perhaps something like this could work" -- upstream's guess).  X(rte) then executes slot+4
   (skipped by the branch) before the target, or the target twice when target == slot+4.  Pre-existing; made reachable by
   ordinary faults by this fix; the diff's comment CERTIFIED it as the contract; table 1's M_DSLOT "kill" produced the MANUAL's
   value (0x10012).  DISPOSITION: do NOT hold the fix (a wrong SFIP in one sub-case beats stopping every CPU; different semantic
   class, untouched code -> its own round by the reopening rule); L4 in-round: comment reworded ("OUTSIDE A DELAY SLOT ... INSIDE
   a delay slot the else-arm is upstream's guess and the manual disagrees, row m88kdslotshadow"); the dslot row LABELLED as
   pinning the arm AS IMPLEMENTED, held for re-pin under the oracle-correction rule when m88kdslotshadow lands; PRETABLE's "M_DSLOT
   (else-arm SFIP wrong)" corrected to "a change detector, not a correctness oracle".  FILED m88kdslotshadow MEDIUM (fix = the
   DATA_ACCESS arm's shape; rows dslot16 + dslotnt; re-pin first).  kimi DISSENTS in part (D1 below).
F15 (grok, Opus, kimi, glm-mini, codex, Fable -- six seats) the tail comment's "ERROR arrives here from the SFRZ re-route with the
   shadows frozen" is false as a universal (errortb: a trap naming 10, shadows updated).  ACCEPTED -> comment names both routes;
   also codex's point that SXIP then names the FIRST fault.
F16 (Opus, Fable) "6.8.3 and 6.6 say the destination register (and carry bit) are unchanged": 6.8.3 says nothing about carry.
   ACCEPTED -> "6.5.x/6.8.3 ... (6.6: and the carry bit, for the overflow case)".
F17 (Opus) the tail `default:` is now DEAD CODE for vectors < 128 (implemented = {0..10} = the tail labels).  ACCEPTED -> one
   sentence in the comment ("unreachable code kept as the tripwire for an eighth class").  M_NAIVE/M_ERRORTAIL re-arm it.
F18 (codex, kimi) "SSBR stays 0" too absolute: the zero is written only under update_shadow_regs; a frozen SSBR keeps its value.
   kimi: "the FP exception registers the fork does not write" slightly overbroad (the SFU1 raise sites DO write FPECR).
   ACCEPTED both -> comment reworded.
F19 (codex, deepseek) F6-of-pass-1's "residual 119 vectors" is the five-class count; seven classes -> 117 (11..113 = 103, 116..127
   = 12, + 114/115).  ACCEPTED -> comment says "117 vectors with the two SFU1 ones"; the CHANGELOG carries the deriver.
F20 (codex, kimi) the "8-of-128" census is wrong (11 of 128 low vectors delivered = 4 pre-existing + 7; 117 halt).  ACCEPTED for
   the records; Fable and kimi could not FIND "8 of 128" in any file (it was the main loop's pass-1 disposition text) -> record the
   corrected census, not a correction of a phantom.
F21 (codex) F15-of-pass-1 cited Figure 6-4 (the rte flow); the recognition diagram is Figure 6-3 and §6.10 prose is the better
   warrant.  ACCEPTED (records).
F22 (Fable 8) the house-idiom sentence ("MIPS/PPC/ARM cores deliver everything they recognise") is wrong for ARM: `arm_exception`
   exits on RESET and on an unrecognised number.  ACCEPTED -> records: "MIPS and PPC deliver unconditionally; SH and ARM exit only
   on RESET-class or unrecognised numbers".
F23 (codex, kimi) a trap while frozen: the common path zeroes DMT0-2/DMD/DMA OUTSIDE the update_shadow_regs guard, while 6.5.5
   says a trap taken while frozen modifies none of the exception-time registers.  Pre-existing, unasserted.  FILED
   m88kexcfrozendmt LOW (the trapfrozen row observes pc and the shadows, not DMT).
F24 (Fable 9d) supervisor tb1 naming 1/2/3 reaches the INTERRUPT/INSTRUCTION_ACCESS/DATA_ACCESS tail arms built for the hardware
   event (SNIP -= 4 -> rte re-executes the trap forever; SNIP = SFIP = 0; DMT from stale dmt[]).  Pre-existing, F4-of-pass-1's
   family (vector 0 named by a trap dies in the RESET tail arm: filed m88kexcresetkill in pass 1 -- CHECK that row exists; the
   ledger grep for `m88kexc` finds only m88kexcdeliver and m88kexcsfu1, so it was NOT filed and the pass-1 record is wrong about
   that).  DISPOSITION: file ONE row m88kexchwtraps LOW covering vectors 0..3 named by a supervisor trap.

## 3. DISAGREEMENTS
D1 THE DELAY-SLOT CONTRACT.  Fable/codex/grok: the else-arm is wrong per Table 6-3 and the pointer definitions (:2097-2104: while
   the SLOT executes, NIP = target, FIP = target+4).  kimi: "The manual's SNIP/SFIP pipeline-shadow semantics at 6.4.3.1.2/.3 do
   support the code's else-arm: SNIP = shadow of NIP, SFIP = 'the target of a jump or branch instruction.'  The pre-existing
   DATA_ACCESS-in-delay-slot arm, SNIP = delay_target, is the odd one out against that reading."  RULING: FILED as m88kdslotshadow
   MEDIUM with kimi's dissent VERBATIM as provenance; the re-pin-first discipline settles it by measurement (the manual's rte flow
   :9023-9050 executes the SNIP instruction then the SFIP one, so the two readings predict DIFFERENT post-rte execution sequences
   -- that is the row).  Parties: kimi vs Fable/codex/grok -- adjudicate your own dispute; the question is WHICH pipeline stage the
   shadows snapshot for a precise fault IN the slot.
D2 M_TAILOFF: Opus "escape -- vectors 0..3 unrowed" vs Fable "equivalent by construction for the seven".  Both true of different
   things: for the seven arms (bare break) the guard is equivalent; for vectors 0..3 it is not, and they are unrowed.  RULING: filed
   in m88kexcdebt as "vectors 0..3 unrowed", disclosed in the contract.
D3 THE INFO LEVEL: Fable 8 says #261's break-on-ERROR is why INFO matters; grok/Opus say the level is sibling-consistent and no
   hygiene row keys on it; codex says "unobservable must mean unobserved by this detector -- verbosity filtering and configured
   subsystem/error breakpoints observe it."  RULING: all compatible; the level IS observable (F7) and INFO is right.  No dispute
   survives -- confirm or refute.

## OPERATIONAL RULE FOR EVERY SEAT (appended by panel.sh, not by the author)

**Do not run `run.sh`, any `gate_*.sh`, or the battery.** Every measurement you need is
inlined above. A gate takes minutes -- longer than your response budget -- and a second
concurrent invocation corrupts the operator run in progress, because the gates share one
log directory. A seat that runs a gate produces no review AND may invalidate someone
else s. Building and running a single detector or mutant in your own temp directory is
fine and encouraged; driving the harness is not.

If you want a measurement that is not inlined, NAME IT and say why. Naming it is worth
far more to this panel than a timeout.

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""m88kexcdeliver -- rung-3 witness AND detector: does the m88k exception core DELIVER a trap-like
exception (vector entry reached, shadow registers right, machine still running), or stop the machine?

`cpu_m88k.c`'s exception switch marks only RESET / INTERRUPT / INSTRUCTION_ACCESS / DATA_ACCESS
`implemented`; every other vector below M88K_EXCEPTION_USER_TRAPS_START falls to a fallback that
prints `unimplemented vector=0x%03x` and sets `running = false` on EVERY guest CPU.  The vector-
specific tail switch then has a `default:` that stops THIS cpu with `internal error` -- so a fix
needs BOTH `implemented = true` AND a tail arm (the naive one-site fix was MEASURED to halt, pass 1).
Real hardware delivers VBR + 8*vector (MC88100 UM Table 6-1, mc88100.txt:8524-8538) and the guest OS
turns it into a signal; rte resumes at SNIP (:8983-8985), the destination register is unchanged
(6.6 :9305-9308; 6.8.3 :10300-10305).

Cold-debugger probe on the COMMITTED `testm88k` machine (no machine or device edits).  Per row: a
guest program at 0x10000 planted with `put w` (liveness marker `addu r9,r0,r10` first), registers
seeded with `rN=`, a `br .` (c0000000) planted at every vector entry so a delivered exception spins
rather than executing zeros, `breakpoint add <8*vector>`, `continue`, then `reg` and `reg 0,0`.

  row        program (after the marker at 0x10000)                 raise                     entry
  div0       divu r2,r3,r0            f4436800                    ILLEGAL_INTEGER_DIVIDE 8  0x40
  ovf        add  r2,r3,r4 (7fffffff+1) f4437004                  INTEGER_OVERFLOW 9        0x48
  misalign   ld   r2,r3,0 (r3 odd)    14430000 (major opcode 5)   MISALIGNED_ACCESS 4       0x20
  bounds     tb1  0,r3,7 (r3=1)       f003d807  supervisor trap   BOUNDS_CHECK 7            0x38
  priv       stcr r3,cr1 (r3=0x2: MODE clear) 80038023; ldcr r2,cr1 80404020   PRIVILEGE 6  0x30
  privtb     stcr r3,cr1; tb1 0,r4,8 (f004d808) in user mode      PRIVILEGE 6               0x30
  unimpl     stcr r3,cr1; prom_call f400fc92 in user mode         UNIMPLEMENTED_OPCODE 5    0x28
  error      div0 whose handler at 0x40 is a misaligned ld: the 2nd fault with SFRZ set re-routes
             to ERROR 10                                                                    0x50
  dslot      br.n +16 (c4000004) with divu r2,r3,r0 in its delay slot -> the else-arm of the
             delay-slot branch (SNIP = pc+4 = 0x1000c, SFIP = delay_target = 0x10014 -- two
             DIFFERENT numbers; with br.n +8 both were 0x1000e and the SFIP channel was vacuous)  ILLEGAL_INTEGER_DIVIDE 0x40
             *** THIS ROW PINS THE ELSE-ARM AS IMPLEMENTED, NOT AS THE MANUAL'S CONTRACT.  Table 6-3 (shadows copy
             XIP/NIP/FIP: for a fault in the delay slot of a taken br.n, NIP = target, FIP = target+4) and X(rte)'s
             `fip != nip + 4' arm say the else-arm returns WRONG (codex, grok, pass 2: the fallthrough executes
             before the target).  Pre-existing, out of this diff's scope, filed as m88kdslotshadow; when that round
             lands this row is RE-PINNED FIRST (oracle-correction rule: re-pin, measure HEAD red, then fix). ***
  errortb    tb1  0,r3,10 (r3=1)      f003d80a  supervisor trap naming ERROR (10) directly -- the
             only route to switch 1's ERROR arm (the double-fault route enters at the re-route AFTER
             switch 1, so it cannot see that arm: M_ERRORDROP escaped the error row 9/9, table 1)  0x50
  misaligntb/unimpltb/div0tb/ovftb   tb1 0,r3,{4,5,8,9} (f003d804/05/08/09), supervisor: the TRAP
             ROUTE into switch 1 for the four classes whose other rows arrive by instruction --
             `if (!is_trap) implemented = true' escapes those rows (grok, deepseek, pass 2)  0x20/0x28/0x40/0x48
  vbrtb      stcr r5,cr7 (800580e5, r5=0x2000) then tb1 0,r3,7: the vector table RELOCATED -> entry
             0x2038, not 0x38.  Every other row runs with VBR == 0, so `pc = 8*vector' (VBR dropped)
             was a 10/10 escape (Opus, pass 2).  The one ANCHORED row: it alone reds if the table moves.  0x2038
  trapfrozen div0 whose handler at 0x40 is tb1 0,r3,7 (r3=0x11): a TRAP with SFRZ already set is NOT
             re-routed to ERROR (UM :8633-8636, 6.10 :10613-10614) -- it delivers to 0x38 with the
             shadows still frozen.  `if (!is_trap)' -> `if (true)' was a 10/10 escape (Opus, pass 2)  0x38
  privsuptb  tb1 0,r3,6 (f003d806) in SUPERVISOR mode: the trap route into PRIVILEGE_VIOLATION.  privtb is
             NOT that route -- the user-mode filter in X(tb0) raises PRIVILEGE with is_trap == 0, so
             `implemented = !is_trap' on the PRIVILEGE arm left priv AND privtb green (codex, glm: pass 2)  0x30
  usertb     tb1 0,r3,128 (f003d880): the first USER trap vector, delivered by the pre-existing code to
             0x400.  Pins the tail switch's `< USER_TRAPS_START' guard: `<=' sends 128 into the tail
             default and stops the cpu (codex's one-character escape, pass 2)                       0x400
  resid50    tb1 0,r3,50 (f003d832): a residual vector.  EXPECTED RESULT = HALT (the fallback, by design).
             Negative control: the shipped form must NOT deliver it (rejected D4 would)             (0x190)
  sfu1h      div0 whose handler at 0x40 is a LEGAL divu r6,r5,r4: with SFD1 set the divide raises
             SFU1_PRECISE (114).  EXPECTED RESULT = HALT: this round does not deliver SFU1 (row
             m88kexcsfu1); a mutant that does turns this row red                                   (0x390)
  udiv0      stcr r3,cr1 (r3=0x2: user mode) then divu r2,r3,r0: the fallback comment's own sentence,
             'one user-mode divu by zero ended the emulation', measured literally; also pins that user mode
             with SFD1 CLEAR yields vector 8, not SFU1 (the m88kexcsfu1 boundary)              ILLEGAL_INTEGER_DIVIDE 0x40
  ovfc       stcr r5,cr1 (80058025, r5=0x90000002: C set) then add 7fffffff+1: the carry bit must
             SURVIVE the exception path (UM 6.6) -> PSR 0x9000000b, EPSR 0x90000002.  Every other
             row's pre-fault C is 0, so PSR == 0x8000000b pinned 'C not set', never 'C unchanged'  0x48
  live       divu r2,r3,r4  (r3=0x60, r4=0x10) -> r2 = 6           none      LIVENESS control
  attrib     divu r2,r3,r4  (r3=0x10, r4=0x10) -> r2 = 1           none      ATTRIBUTION control
  selftest   no program: `reg 0,0` at reset must parse PSR == 0x80000002 (the CR parse works)

RESULT per fault row (the classifier tests the STOP channel before pc, deliberately: the naive fix
sets pc = entry and THEN stops, so pc alone cannot tell it from delivery):
  HALT       `unimplemented vector=` printed and the machine stopped (pre-fix)
  TAILHALT   `internal error` printed and the machine stopped (the naive fix's shape)
  STOP       the machine stopped for another reason (e.g. a wrong vector base landed in zeros)
  BADREGS    the entry breakpoint fired but a shadow-register/PSR/destination assert failed, or
             (bad=level) the transcript carried an `exception <CLASS>' line: the class printed at ERROR
             level, which the fork's default WARNING verbosity shows and INFO does not (kills M_LEVEL)
  (rows resid50 and sfu1h EXPECT HALT: the fallback stopping the machine is the correct answer there)
  DELIVER    entry breakpoint fired, machine running, and every assert holds:
             pc == entry, SXIP == fault_pc|V, SNIP, SFIP, PSR == 0x8000000b (SFRZ|IND|SFD1|MODE),
             EPSR == the pre-fault PSR (0x80000002 supervisor rows; 0x00000002 user-mode rows),
             r2 unchanged (0xdeadbeef)
  NOSTART    the liveness marker never ran
Controls print DONE (r2 == the expected quotient, machine running) or a failure word.

Output: one `M88KEXC_ROW=<name> RESULT=<word> ...` line per row, `M88KEXC_RESULT=<delivered>/<fault rows>`,
`M88KEXC_SELFTEST=OK|FAIL`, `M88KEXC_DONE`.  Set M88KEXC_VERBOSE=1 for transcripts.

CONTRACT (clause e): catches a class returned to the fallback (HALT) by either route (instruction rows
and the *tb trap rows); a class reaching the tail default (TAILHALT); a wrong vector SCALING (M_VEC4,
M_VEC7: STOP) and a DROPPED vector base (vbrtb: STOP); a stale SXIP/SNIP/SFIP; a wrong PSR, including a
cleared carry (ovfc); the debugmsg LEVEL (bad=level on any delivered row: ERROR prints at default verbosity, INFO
does not -- M_LEVEL); a skipped EPSR save; a skipped supervisor switch (the user-mode rows: EPSR MODE
clear vs PSR MODE set); a wrong return address; the delay-slot else-arm's SFIP source (dslot, +16
geometry); a trap re-routed to ERROR while frozen (trapfrozen); a written destination.  Does NOT catch:
fcr2/fcr4/fcr5; scoreboard (SSBR) bits; DMT contents; the post-delivery
`cpu->delay_slot' write on the NON-delay path (M_DELAYALWAYS: needs a second UNFROZEN exception -- filed);
vectors 0..3's tail arms (no row: M_TAILOFF -- filed); vectors 11..113 and 116..127 (fallback halts by
design); SFU1 (114/115, row m88kexcsfu1); `implemented = is_trap' on BOUNDS/ERROR (EQUIVALENT today: no
instruction raises 7 or 10 -- becomes live when tbnd lands, row m88ktbnd).
"""
import os, pty, re, select, struct, sys, time

BIN = sys.argv[1] if len(sys.argv) > 1 else "/tmp/gxsec-build/gxemul"
CODE = 0x10000
DATA = 0x20000
XIP_V = 0x2
PSR_RESET = 0x80000002          # MODE | IND (cpu_m88k.c: cr[PSR] = M88K_PSR_MODE | M88K_PSR_IND)
PSR_EXC = 0x8000000b            # MODE | SFD1 | IND | SFRZ after the common path
PSR_USER = 0x00000002           # IND only: what the user-mode rows write via stcr

def tri(sub, d, s1, s2):
    return 0xf4000000 | (d << 21) | (s1 << 16) | (sub << 8) | s2

MARK = tri(0x60, 9, 0, 10)                       # addu r9,r0,r10  -> r9 = 0x12345678 (liveness)
SELF = 0xc0000000                                # br . (disp 0)
DIVU_R2_R3_R0 = tri(0x68, 2, 3, 0)               # f4436800
DIVU_R2_R3_R4 = tri(0x68, 2, 3, 4)               # f4436804
DIVU_R6_R5_R4 = tri(0x68, 6, 5, 4)               # f4c56804 -- a LEGAL divide inside a handler: SFD1 is set there, so X(divu) raises
                                                 #   SFU1_PRECISE (114) before it looks at the divisor -> the fallback must still HALT
ADD_R2_R3_R4 = tri(0x70, 2, 3, 4)                # f4437004
LD_R2_R3_0 = 0x14000000 | (2 << 21) | (3 << 16)  # 14430000 (major opcode 5, ld)
TB1_0_R3_7 = 0xf003d807                          # tb1 0,r3,7   (computed vector 7, supervisor)
TB1_0_R4_8 = 0xf004d808                          # tb1 0,r4,8
TB1_0_R3_10 = 0xf003d80a
TB1_0_R3_4 = 0xf003d804                          # tb1 0,r3,4   -- a supervisor trap naming MISALIGNED_ACCESS (4) directly
TB1_0_R3_5 = 0xf003d805                          # tb1 0,r3,5   -- naming UNIMPLEMENTED_OPCODE (5)
TB1_0_R3_8 = 0xf003d808                          # tb1 0,r3,8   -- naming ILLEGAL_INTEGER_DIVIDE (8)
TB1_0_R3_9 = 0xf003d809                          # tb1 0,r3,9   -- naming INTEGER_OVERFLOW (9)
TB1_0_R3_6 = 0xf003d806                          # tb1 0,r3,6   -- naming PRIVILEGE_VIOLATION (6): the user-mode tb1 filter raises
                                                 #   PRIVILEGE with is_trap == 0 (cpu_m88k_instr.c X(tb0)), so privtb is NOT the trap route
TB1_0_R3_128 = 0xf003d880                        # tb1 0,r3,128 -- the first USER trap vector: delivered by the pre-existing code (entry 0x400)
TB1_0_R3_50 = 0xf003d832                         # tb1 0,r3,50  -- a RESIDUAL vector (11..113): must still HALT (negative control vs D4)
#  The four *tb rows are the TRAP ROUTE into switch 1 for the classes whose other rows arrive by INSTRUCTION
#  (is_trap == 0).  Pass-2 seats (grok, deepseek, independently) predicted `if (!is_trap) implemented = true;'
#  escapes the instruction rows for MISALIGNED/UNIMPL/DIV0/OVF; these rows are the ones that redden it (M_ISTRAP).
                         # tb1 0,r3,10  (a supervisor trap naming vector 10 = ERROR directly)
STCR_R3_CR1 = 0x80038023                         # stcr r3,cr1 (PSR)
STCR_R5_CR1 = 0x80058025                         # stcr r5,cr1 (PSR)  -- same format: 0x80000000 | S1<<16 | 0x8000 | CR<<5 | S1
STCR_R5_CR7 = 0x800580e5                         # stcr r5,cr7 (VBR)
PSR_CARRY = 0x90000002                           # MODE | C | IND: the ovfc row's pre-fault PSR (C = bit 28, UM 6.6 'carry bit unchanged')
VBR_TEST = 0x2000                                # the vbrtb row relocates the vector table here (OpenBSD relocates its own)
LDCR_R2_CR1 = 0x80404020                         # ldcr r2,cr1
PROM_CALL = 0xf400fc92                           # M88K_PROM_INSTR
BR_N_PLUS8 = 0xc4000002
BR_N_PLUS16 = 0xc4000004                         # br.n +16: delay_target (slot+8) != slot_pc+4, so SNIP and SFIP are DIFFERENT numbers                          # br.n +2 words

V_MISALIGN, V_UNIMPL, V_PRIV, V_BOUNDS, V_DIV0, V_OVF, V_ERROR = 4, 5, 6, 7, 8, 9, 10
V_SFU1 = 114
V_USER0 = 128
V_RESID = 50
VECTORS = (V_MISALIGN, V_UNIMPL, V_PRIV, V_BOUNDS, V_DIV0, V_OVF, V_ERROR, V_SFU1, V_USER0, V_RESID)

# name, program words after MARK, regs, vector, fault_pc, epsr, handler words at the div0 entry (None = br .)
ROWS = [
    ("div0",     [DIVU_R2_R3_R0],               {"r3": 0x10},                    V_DIV0,     0x10004, PSR_RESET, None),
    ("ovf",      [ADD_R2_R3_R4],                {"r3": 0x7fffffff, "r4": 1},     V_OVF,      0x10004, PSR_RESET, None),
    ("misalign", [LD_R2_R3_0],                  {"r3": DATA + 1},                V_MISALIGN, 0x10004, PSR_RESET, None),
    ("bounds",   [TB1_0_R3_7],                  {"r3": 1},                       V_BOUNDS,   0x10004, PSR_RESET, None),
    ("priv",     [STCR_R3_CR1, LDCR_R2_CR1],    {"r3": PSR_USER},                V_PRIV,     0x10008, PSR_USER,  None),
    ("privtb",   [STCR_R3_CR1, TB1_0_R4_8],     {"r3": PSR_USER, "r4": 1},       V_PRIV,     0x10008, PSR_USER,  None),
    ("unimpl",   [STCR_R3_CR1, PROM_CALL],      {"r3": PSR_USER},                V_UNIMPL,   0x10008, PSR_USER,  None),
    ("error",    [DIVU_R2_R3_R0],               {"r3": 0x10, "r5": DATA + 1},    V_ERROR,    0x10004, PSR_RESET,
                 [0x14000000 | (6 << 21) | (5 << 16)]),       # handler at 0x40: ld r6,r5,0 with r5 odd -> 2nd fault
    ("dslot",    [BR_N_PLUS16, DIVU_R2_R3_R0],  {"r3": 0x10},                    V_DIV0,     0x10008, PSR_RESET, None),
    ("errortb",  [TB1_0_R3_10],                 {"r3": 1},                       V_ERROR,    0x10004, PSR_RESET, None),
    ("misaligntb", [TB1_0_R3_4],                {"r3": 1},                       V_MISALIGN, 0x10004, PSR_RESET, None),
    ("unimpltb", [TB1_0_R3_5],                  {"r3": 1},                       V_UNIMPL,   0x10004, PSR_RESET, None),
    ("div0tb",   [TB1_0_R3_8],                  {"r3": 1},                       V_DIV0,     0x10004, PSR_RESET, None),
    ("ovftb",    [TB1_0_R3_9],                  {"r3": 1},                       V_OVF,      0x10004, PSR_RESET, None),
    ("vbrtb",    [STCR_R5_CR7, TB1_0_R3_7],     {"r3": 1, "r5": VBR_TEST},       V_BOUNDS,   0x10008, PSR_RESET, None),
    ("trapfrozen", [DIVU_R2_R3_R0],             {"r3": 0x11},                    V_BOUNDS,   0x10004, PSR_RESET,
                 [TB1_0_R3_7]),                               # handler at 0x40: tb1 0,r3,7 with SFRZ set -> a TRAP is not re-routed to ERROR
    ("ovfc",     [STCR_R5_CR1, ADD_R2_R3_R4],   {"r3": 0x7fffffff, "r4": 1, "r5": PSR_CARRY}, V_OVF, 0x10008, PSR_CARRY, None),
    ("privsuptb", [TB1_0_R3_6],                 {"r3": 1},                       V_PRIV,     0x10004, PSR_RESET, None),
    ("udiv0",    [STCR_R3_CR1, DIVU_R2_R3_R0],  {"r3": PSR_USER},                V_DIV0,     0x10008, PSR_USER,  None),
    ("usertb",   [TB1_0_R3_128],                {"r3": 1},                       V_USER0,    0x10004, PSR_RESET, None),
    ("resid50",  [TB1_0_R3_50],                 {"r3": 1},                       V_RESID,    0x10004, PSR_RESET, None),
    ("sfu1h",    [DIVU_R2_R3_R0],               {"r3": 0x10, "r5": 0x60, "r4": 0x10}, V_SFU1, 0x10004, PSR_RESET,
                 [DIVU_R6_R5_R4]),                             # handler at 0x40: a legal divu with SFD1 set -> SFU1 (114) -> fallback HALT
    ("live",     [DIVU_R2_R3_R4],               {"r3": 0x60, "r4": 0x10},        None,       None,    None,      None),
    ("attrib",   [DIVU_R2_R3_R4],               {"r3": 0x10, "r4": 0x10},        None,       None,    None,      None),
]
EXPECT_R2 = {"live": 6, "attrib": 1}
VBR_OF = {"vbrtb": VBR_TEST}                     # rows that relocate the vector table; every other row runs with VBR == 0
#  NEGATIVE CONTROLS: rows whose CORRECT result is the fallback HALT.  They pin the boundary the diff keeps on purpose --
#  vectors 11..113/116..127 and SFU1 (114/115) still stop the machine -- and separate the shipped D1 form from the rejected
#  D4 (tail-default-delivers) and from delivering SFU1 (row m88kexcsfu1).  A mutant that delivers them turns these rows RED.
EXPECT_RESULT = {"resid50": "HALT", "sfu1h": "HALT"}
PSR_WANT = {"ovfc": 0x9000000b}                  # MODE | C | SFD1 | IND | SFRZ: carry PRESERVED through the exception path

class Sess:
    def __init__(self):
        stub = "/tmp/gx_m88k_exc_%d.bin" % os.getpid()
        with open(stub, "wb") as f:
            f.write(struct.pack(">I", 0xf4005800))          # or r0,r0,r0 so the machine constructs
        self.pid, self.fd = pty.fork()
        if self.pid == 0:
            os.execvp(BIN, [BIN, "-V", "-E", "testm88k", "-M", "64", "0x10000:%s" % stub])
            os._exit(127)
        self.buf = ""
    def rd(self, t=0.4):
        r, _, _ = select.select([self.fd], [], [], t)
        if self.fd not in r:
            return True
        try:
            d = os.read(self.fd, 65536)
        except OSError:
            return False
        if not d:
            return False
        self.buf += d.decode("latin1", "replace")
        return True
    def wait_prompt(self, mark, timeout=30):
        t = time.time()
        while time.time() - t < timeout:
            if not self.rd():
                return False
            if len(self.buf) > mark and self.buf[mark:].rstrip().endswith("GXemul>"):
                return True
        return False
    def send(self, sx, timeout=30):
        mark = len(self.buf)
        b = (sx + "\n").encode("latin1"); n = 0
        while n < len(b):
            n += os.write(self.fd, b[n:])
        self.wait_prompt(mark, timeout)
        return mark
    def cr(self, name):
        m = self.send("reg 0,0")
        mm = re.search(r"(?<![A-Z])%s=0x([0-9a-f]{8})" % name, self.buf[m:])
        return int(mm.group(1), 16) if mm else None
    def gpr(self, n):
        m = self.send("reg")
        mm = re.search(r"r%-2d\s*=\s*0x([0-9a-f]{8})" % n, self.buf[m:])
        return int(mm.group(1), 16) if mm else None
    def pc(self):
        m = self.send("reg")
        mm = re.search(r"pc\s*=\s*0x([0-9a-f]{8})", self.buf[m:])
        return int(mm.group(1), 16) if mm else None
    def close(self):
        try:
            os.write(self.fd, b"quit\n"); time.sleep(0.3); os.kill(self.pid, 9); os.waitpid(self.pid, 0)
        except Exception:
            pass

def fmt(v):
    return ("0x%08x" % v) if isinstance(v, int) else str(v)

def run_row(name, prog, regs, vector, fault_pc, epsr_exp, handler, grace=6.0):
    s = Sess()
    if not s.wait_prompt(0, 60):
        s.close(); return dict(result="NOBOOT")
    vbr = VBR_OF.get(name, 0)
    entry = (vbr + 8 * vector) if vector is not None else None

8898:6.4.3.1.2 Shadow Fetch Instruction Pointer (SFIP). The SFIP is a 32-bit register that shad-
8899-ows the FIP when shadowing is enabled. When a precise exception occurs, the SFIP contains
8900-a pointer either to the instruction following the next instruction or to the target of a jump
8901-or branch instruction. An rte instruction fetches the instruction pointed to by the SNIP and
8902-then the instruction pointed to by the SFIP.
8903-
8904-The SFIP contains a valid (V) and an exception (E) bit. The V bit can be cleared by software,
8905-effectively making the corresponding instruction a no-operation instruction when an rte is
8906-executed. In this case, the corresponding instruction is not prefetched from memory. If
8907-both the V and E bits are set, an rte instruction causes the instruction pointed to by the
8908-SFIP to generate an instruction access exception when it advances to the NIP, regardless
8909-of whether the transaction receives a 'success' or 'fault' reply from the memory system.
8910-An rte instruction can be made to return to a particular instruction by placing a valid

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""m88kexcdeliver -- rung-3 witness AND detector: does the m88k exception core DELIVER a trap-like
exception (vector entry reached, shadow registers right, machine still running), or stop the machine?

`cpu_m88k.c`'s exception switch marks only RESET / INTERRUPT / INSTRUCTION_ACCESS / DATA_ACCESS
`implemented`; every other vector below M88K_EXCEPTION_USER_TRAPS_START falls to a fallback that
prints `unimplemented vector=0x%03x` and sets `running = false` on EVERY guest CPU.  The vector-
specific tail switch then has a `default:` that stops THIS cpu with `internal error` -- so a fix
needs BOTH `implemented = true` AND a tail arm (the naive one-site fix was MEASURED to halt, pass 1).
Real hardware delivers VBR + 8*vector (MC88100 UM Table 6-1, mc88100.txt:8524-8538) and the guest OS
turns it into a signal; rte resumes at SNIP (:8983-8985), the destination register is unchanged
(6.6 :9305-9308; 6.8.3 :10300-10305).

Cold-debugger probe on the COMMITTED `testm88k` machine (no machine or device edits).  Per row: a
guest program at 0x10000 planted with `put w` (liveness marker `addu r9,r0,r10` first), registers
seeded with `rN=`, a `br .` (c0000000) planted at every vector entry so a delivered exception spins
rather than executing zeros, `breakpoint add <8*vector>`, `continue`, then `reg` and `reg 0,0`.

  row        program (after the marker at 0x10000)                 raise                     entry
  div0       divu r2,r3,r0            f4436800                    ILLEGAL_INTEGER_DIVIDE 8  0x40
  ovf        add  r2,r3,r4 (7fffffff+1) f4437004                  INTEGER_OVERFLOW 9        0x48
  misalign   ld   r2,r3,0 (r3 odd)    14430000 (major opcode 5)   MISALIGNED_ACCESS 4       0x20
  bounds     tb1  0,r3,7 (r3=1)       f003d807  supervisor trap   BOUNDS_CHECK 7            0x38
  priv       stcr r3,cr1 (r3=0x2: MODE clear) 80038023; ldcr r2,cr1 80404020   PRIVILEGE 6  0x30
  privtb     stcr r3,cr1; tb1 0,r4,8 (f004d808) in user mode      PRIVILEGE 6               0x30
  unimpl     stcr r3,cr1; prom_call f400fc92 in user mode         UNIMPLEMENTED_OPCODE 5    0x28
  error      div0 whose handler at 0x40 is a misaligned ld: the 2nd fault with SFRZ set re-routes
             to ERROR 10                                                                    0x50
  dslot      br.n +16 (c4000004) with divu r2,r3,r0 in its delay slot -> the else-arm of the
             delay-slot branch (SNIP = pc+4 = 0x1000c, SFIP = delay_target = 0x10014 -- two
             DIFFERENT numbers; with br.n +8 both were 0x1000e and the SFIP channel was vacuous)  ILLEGAL_INTEGER_DIVIDE 0x40
             *** THIS ROW PINS THE ELSE-ARM AS IMPLEMENTED, NOT AS THE MANUAL'S CONTRACT.  Table 6-3 (shadows copy
             XIP/NIP/FIP: for a fault in the delay slot of a taken br.n, NIP = target, FIP = target+4) and X(rte)'s
             `fip != nip + 4' arm say the else-arm returns WRONG (codex, grok, pass 2: the fallthrough executes
             before the target).  Pre-existing, out of this diff's scope, filed as m88kdslotshadow; when that round
             lands this row is RE-PINNED FIRST (oracle-correction rule: re-pin, measure HEAD red, then fix). ***
  errortb    tb1  0,r3,10 (r3=1)      f003d80a  supervisor trap naming ERROR (10) directly -- the
             only route to switch 1's ERROR arm (the double-fault route enters at the re-route AFTER
             switch 1, so it cannot see that arm: M_ERRORDROP escaped the error row 9/9, table 1)  0x50
  misaligntb/unimpltb/div0tb/ovftb   tb1 0,r3,{4,5,8,9} (f003d804/05/08/09), supervisor: the TRAP
             ROUTE into switch 1 for the four classes whose other rows arrive by instruction --
             `if (!is_trap) implemented = true' escapes those rows (grok, deepseek, pass 2)  0x20/0x28/0x40/0x48
  vbrtb      stcr r5,cr7 (800580e5, r5=0x2000) then tb1 0,r3,7: the vector table RELOCATED -> entry
             0x2038, not 0x38.  Every other row runs with VBR == 0, so `pc = 8*vector' (VBR dropped)
             was a 10/10 escape (Opus, pass 2).  The one ANCHORED row: it alone reds if the table moves.  0x2038
  trapfrozen div0 whose handler at 0x40 is tb1 0,r3,7 (r3=0x11): a TRAP with SFRZ already set is NOT
             re-routed to ERROR (UM :8633-8636, 6.10 :10613-10614) -- it delivers to 0x38 with the
             shadows still frozen.  `if (!is_trap)' -> `if (true)' was a 10/10 escape (Opus, pass 2)  0x38
  privsuptb  tb1 0,r3,6 (f003d806) in SUPERVISOR mode: the trap route into PRIVILEGE_VIOLATION.  privtb is
             NOT that route -- the user-mode filter in X(tb0) raises PRIVILEGE with is_trap == 0, so
             `implemented = !is_trap' on the PRIVILEGE arm left priv AND privtb green (codex, glm: pass 2)  0x30
  usertb     tb1 0,r3,128 (f003d880): the first USER trap vector, delivered by the pre-existing code to
             0x400.  Pins the tail switch's `< USER_TRAPS_START' guard: `<=' sends 128 into the tail
             default and stops the cpu (codex's one-character escape, pass 2)                       0x400
  resid50    tb1 0,r3,50 (f003d832): a residual vector.  EXPECTED RESULT = HALT (the fallback, by design).
             Negative control: the shipped form must NOT deliver it (rejected D4 would)             (0x190)
  sfu1h      div0 whose handler at 0x40 is a LEGAL divu r6,r5,r4: with SFD1 set the divide raises
             SFU1_PRECISE (114).  EXPECTED RESULT = HALT: this round does not deliver SFU1 (row
             m88kexcsfu1); a mutant that does turns this row red                                   (0x390)
  udiv0      stcr r3,cr1 (r3=0x2: user mode) then divu r2,r3,r0: the fallback comment's own sentence,
             'one user-mode divu by zero ended the emulation', measured literally; also pins that user mode
             with SFD1 CLEAR yields vector 8, not SFU1 (the m88kexcsfu1 boundary)              ILLEGAL_INTEGER_DIVIDE 0x40
  ovfc       stcr r5,cr1 (80058025, r5=0x90000002: C set) then add 7fffffff+1: the carry bit must
             SURVIVE the exception path (UM 6.6) -> PSR 0x9000000b, EPSR 0x90000002.  Every other
             row's pre-fault C is 0, so PSR == 0x8000000b pinned 'C not set', never 'C unchanged'  0x48
  live       divu r2,r3,r4  (r3=0x60, r4=0x10) -> r2 = 6           none      LIVENESS control
  attrib     divu r2,r3,r4  (r3=0x10, r4=0x10) -> r2 = 1           none      ATTRIBUTION control
  selftest   no program: `reg 0,0` at reset must parse PSR == 0x80000002 (the CR parse works)

RESULT per fault row (the classifier tests the STOP channel before pc, deliberately: the naive fix
sets pc = entry and THEN stops, so pc alone cannot tell it from delivery):
  HALT       `unimplemented vector=` printed and the machine stopped (pre-fix)
  TAILHALT   `internal error` printed and the machine stopped (the naive fix's shape)
  STOP       the machine stopped for another reason (e.g. a wrong vector base landed in zeros)
  BADREGS    the entry breakpoint fired but a shadow-register/PSR/destination assert failed, or
             (bad=level) the transcript carried an `exception <CLASS>' line: the class printed at ERROR
             level, which the fork's default WARNING verbosity shows and INFO does not (kills M_LEVEL)
  (rows resid50 and sfu1h EXPECT HALT: the fallback stopping the machine is the correct answer there)
  DELIVER    entry breakpoint fired, machine running, and every assert holds:
             pc == entry, SXIP == fault_pc|V, SNIP, SFIP, PSR == 0x8000000b (SFRZ|IND|SFD1|MODE),
             EPSR == the pre-fault PSR (0x80000002 supervisor rows; 0x00000002 user-mode rows),
             r2 unchanged (0xdeadbeef)
  NOSTART    the liveness marker never ran
Controls print DONE (r2 == the expected quotient, machine running) or a failure word.

Output: one `M88KEXC_ROW=<name> RESULT=<word> ...` line per row, `M88KEXC_RESULT=<delivered>/<fault rows>`,
`M88KEXC_SELFTEST=OK|FAIL`, `M88KEXC_DONE`.  Set M88KEXC_VERBOSE=1 for transcripts.

CONTRACT (clause e): catches a class returned to the fallback (HALT) by either route (instruction rows
and the *tb trap rows); a class reaching the tail default (TAILHALT); a wrong vector SCALING (M_VEC4,
M_VEC7: STOP) and a DROPPED vector base (vbrtb: STOP); a stale SXIP/SNIP/SFIP; a wrong PSR, including a
cleared carry (ovfc); the debugmsg LEVEL (bad=level on any delivered row: ERROR prints at default verbosity, INFO
does not -- M_LEVEL); a skipped EPSR save; a skipped supervisor switch (the user-mode rows: EPSR MODE
clear vs PSR MODE set); a wrong return address; the delay-slot else-arm's SFIP source (dslot, +16
geometry); a trap re-routed to ERROR while frozen (trapfrozen); a written destination.  Does NOT catch:
fcr2/fcr4/fcr5; scoreboard (SSBR) bits; DMT contents; the post-delivery
`cpu->delay_slot' write on the NON-delay path (M_DELAYALWAYS: needs a second UNFROZEN exception -- filed);
vectors 0..3's tail arms (no row: M_TAILOFF -- filed); vectors 11..113 and 116..127 (fallback halts by
design); SFU1 (114/115, row m88kexcsfu1); `implemented = is_trap' on BOUNDS/ERROR (EQUIVALENT today: no
instruction raises 7 or 10 -- becomes live when tbnd lands, row m88ktbnd).
"""
import os, pty, re, select, struct, sys, time
    return ("0x%08x" % v) if isinstance(v, int) else str(v)

def run_row(name, prog, regs, vector, fault_pc, epsr_exp, handler, grace=6.0):
    s = Sess()
    if not s.wait_prompt(0, 60):
        s.close(); return dict(result="NOBOOT")
    vbr = VBR_OF.get(name, 0)
    entry = (vbr + 8 * vector) if vector is not None else None
    words = [MARK] + prog + [SELF]
    for i, w in enumerate(words):
        s.send("put w 0x%x, 0x%08x" % (CODE + 4 * i, w))
    for base in sorted({0, vbr}):                       # every entry spins unless a handler is planted (both tables if relocated)
        for v in VECTORS:
            s.send("put w 0x%x, 0x%08x" % (base + 8 * v, SELF))
    if entry is not None:
        s.send("put w 0x%x, 0x%08x" % (entry, SELF))
    if handler:
        for i, w in enumerate(handler):
            s.send("put w 0x%x, 0x%08x" % (8 * V_DIV0 + 4 * i, w))
        s.send("put w 0x%x, 0x%08x" % (8 * V_DIV0 + 4 * len(handler), SELF))
    s.send("put w 0x%x, 0x%08x" % (DATA, 0x0badf00d))
    for r, v in regs.items():
        s.send("%s=0x%x" % (r, v))
    s.send("r10=0x12345678"); s.send("r2=0xdeadbeef")
    if vector is not None:
        s.send("breakpoint add 0x%x" % entry)
    s.send("pc=0x%x" % CODE)
    mark = len(s.buf)
    os.write(s.fd, b"continue\n")
    t = time.time()
    while time.time() - t < grace:
        s.rd(0.3)
        if s.buf[mark:].rstrip().endswith("GXemul>"):
            break
    if not s.buf[mark:].rstrip().endswith("GXemul>"):
        os.write(s.fd, b"\x03"); s.wait_prompt(len(s.buf), 15)
    tr = s.buf[mark:]
    halted = ("unimplemented" in tr and "vector=" in tr)
    #  LEVEL channel (Fable review seat, pass 2): main.c lowers every subsystem to WARNING after startup, so a
    #  free-running `continue' prints an ERROR-level `[ exception <CLASS>: ]' line and stays silent at INFO.  A
    #  delivered row that carries the line has the pre-fix level (M_LEVEL) -- #261's opt-in break-on-ERROR would
    #  then stop the emulator into the debugger on a user process's divide by zero.
    levelmsg = re.search(r"exception (MISALIGNED_ACCESS|UNIMPLEMENTED_OPCODE|PRIVILEGE_VIOLATION|"
                         r"BOUNDS_CHECK_VIOLATION|ILLEGAL_INTEGER_DIVIDE|INTEGER_OVERFLOW|ERROR)", tr) is not None
    tailhalt = "internal error" in tr
    stopped = "machines stopped" in tr
    pc = s.pc(); r2 = s.gpr(2); r9 = s.gpr(9)
    sxip, snip, sfip, psr, epsr = s.cr("SXIP"), s.cr("SNIP"), s.cr("SFIP"), s.cr("PSR"), s.cr("EPSR")
    s.close()
    started = (r9 == 0x12345678)
    res = dict(pc=pc, sxip=sxip, snip=snip, sfip=sfip, psr=psr, epsr=epsr, r2=r2, transcript=tr)
    if not started:
        res["result"] = "NOSTART"; return res
    if vector is None:
        ok = (not stopped) and r2 == EXPECT_R2[name]
        res["result"] = "DONE" if ok else ("STOP" if stopped else "BADREGS"); return res
    if halted:
        res["result"] = "HALT"
    elif tailhalt:
        res["result"] = "TAILHALT"
    elif stopped:
        res["result"] = "STOP"
    elif pc != entry:
        res["result"] = "STOP"          # ran past its grace without the entry breakpoint firing
    else:
        want = dict(sxip=(fault_pc | XIP_V), psr=PSR_WANT.get(name, PSR_EXC), epsr=epsr_exp, r2=0xdeadbeef)
        if name in ("error", "trapfrozen"):
            # the second fault runs with SFRZ set: the shadows stay FROZEN at the first fault's values.  For
            # `trapfrozen' the second fault is a TRAP, which the manual (:8633-8636, 6.10 :10613-10614) exempts from
            # the ERROR re-route: it must deliver to ITS OWN vector (0x38), shadows still frozen.  M_SFRZTRAP sends it to 0x50.
            want.update(sxip=(0x10004 | XIP_V), snip=(0x10008 | XIP_V), sfip=(0x1000c | XIP_V), epsr=PSR_RESET)
        elif name == "dslot":
            # the else-arm of the delay-slot branch: SNIP = (pc + 4) | V, SFIP = delay_target | V.  br.n +16 puts
            # delay_target at 0x10014 while slot_pc+4 is 0x1000c, so the two channels are DIFFERENT numbers (with
            # br.n +8 they were both 0x1000e and M_SFIPNOTGT / M_DSLOTSWAP escaped 10/10 -- Opus, pass 2).
            want.update(snip=((fault_pc + 4) | XIP_V), sfip=((CODE + 4 + 16) | XIP_V))
            assert want["snip"] != want["sfip"], "dslot geometry collapsed: SNIP and SFIP would be one number"
        else:
            want.update(snip=((fault_pc + 4) | XIP_V), sfip=((fault_pc + 8) | XIP_V))
        bad = [k for k, v in want.items() if res.get(k) != v]
        if levelmsg:
            bad.append("level")
        res["result"] = "DELIVER" if not bad else "BADREGS"
        res["bad"] = bad
    return res

def selftest():
    s = Sess()
    ok = s.wait_prompt(0, 60)
    psr = s.cr("PSR") if ok else None
    s.close()
    return psr == PSR_RESET, psr

def main():
    ok, psr = selftest()
    print("M88KEXC_SELFTEST=%s psr_at_reset=%s (want 0x80000002)" % ("OK" if ok else "FAIL", fmt(psr)), flush=True)
    delivered = 0; total = 0; controls_bad = []
    for name, prog, regs, vector, fault_pc, epsr_exp, handler in ROWS:
        res = run_row(name, prog, regs, vector, fault_pc, epsr_exp, handler)
        print("M88KEXC_ROW=%-9s RESULT=%-8s pc=%s sxip=%s snip=%s sfip=%s psr=%s epsr=%s r2=%s%s" % (
            name, res.get("result"), fmt(res.get("pc")), fmt(res.get("sxip")), fmt(res.get("snip")),
            fmt(res.get("sfip")), fmt(res.get("psr")), fmt(res.get("epsr")), fmt(res.get("r2")),
            (" bad=" + ",".join(res["bad"])) if res.get("bad") else ""), flush=True)
        if vector is not None:
            total += 1
            if res.get("result") == EXPECT_RESULT.get(name, "DELIVER"):
                delivered += 1
        elif res.get("result") != "DONE":
            controls_bad.append(name)
        if os.environ.get("M88KEXC_VERBOSE"):
            print("---- transcript ----"); print(res.get("transcript", "")[-1200:]); print("--------------------")
    print("M88KEXC_RESULT=%d/%d" % (delivered, total), flush=True)
    if controls_bad:
        print("M88KEXC_CONTROLS=BAD %s" % ",".join(controls_bad), flush=True)
    print("M88KEXC_DONE", flush=True)
    #  exit status: the gate can rely on it (a row printing a failure word while the process exits 0 was a pass-2 finding)
    sys.exit(0 if (ok and delivered == total and not controls_bad) else 1)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

8622:
8623:For all exceptions except reset and the error exception, the MC88100 and software handler
8624:perform standard procedures for exception processing. These procedures are to recognize
8625:the exception, save the processor context, service the exception, and return to normal
8626:processing after the exception.
8627:
8628:The MC88100 does not automatically save the processor context to memory as part of
8629:exception processing; the processor context is stored in appropriate shadow and exception-
8630:time registers. Saving the necessary context to memory is the responsibility of the software
8631:exception handler.
8632:
8633:The processor is in the exception processing state when shadowing is frozen (SFRZ bit of
8634:the PSR is set). When the MC88100 is in the exception processing state, it cannot process
8635:another exception except for trap instructions. If another exception occurs (other than a
8636:trap instruction), the MC88100 takes the error exception, which is a fatal error. In systems
8637:requiring nested exceptions, shadowing must be enabled as soon as possible after an
8638:exception is recognized to place the processor in the normal state. This allows subsequent
8639:exceptions to occur and be processed properly. Shadowing can be enabled as soon as the
8640:shadow registers required for handling the exception are saved into general-purpose reg-
8641:isters or memory. See 6.10 ERROR EXCEPTION (VECTOR OFFSET $50) for more information

8893:
8894:After an MC88100 reset, the contents of SSBR are not defined. The SSBR must
8895:be explicitly cleared before shadowing is enabled. This guarantees that the SSBR
8896:reflects the SB when shadowing is enabled.
8897:
8898:6.4.3.1.2 Shadow Fetch Instruction Pointer (SFIP). The SFIP is a 32-bit register that shad-
8899:ows the FIP when shadowing is enabled. When a precise exception occurs, the SFIP contains
8900:a pointer either to the instruction following the next instruction or to the target of a jump
8901:or branch instruction. An rte instruction fetches the instruction pointed to by the SNIP and
8902:then the instruction pointed to by the SFIP.
8903:
8904:The SFIP contains a valid (V) and an exception (E) bit. The V bit can be cleared by software,
8905:effectively making the corresponding instruction a no-operation instruction when an rte is
8906:executed. In this case, the corresponding instruction is not prefetched from memory. If
8907:both the V and E bits are set, an rte instruction causes the instruction pointed to by the
8908:SFIP to generate an instruction access exception when it advances to the NIP, regardless
8909:of whether the transaction receives a 'success' or 'fault' reply from the memory system.
8910:An rte instruction can be made to return to a particular instruction by placing a valid
8911:instruction address in the SNIP and the next sequential instruction address in the SFIP
8912:(with V bits set and E bits clear). The rte resumes execution at the instruction pointed to
8913:by the SNIP, then the SFIP. This register has read/write access.

8916:                                                                  SHADOW OF FIP

8918:          SFIP                                                                   cr6

8920:Bits\31-2 - Shadow of Fetch Instruction Pointer
8921:
8922:V-Valid
8923:      0 - Fetch instruction is not valid (corresponds to no-operation); ignore E bit.
8924:      1 - Fetch instruction is valid.

8926:E - Exception
8927:      0 - Fetch instruction is fetched normally.
8928:      1 - Force instruction access fault if execution of the instruction is attempted.

8930:MOTOROLA  MC88100 USER'S MANUAL                                                            6-11
8931:6.4.3.1.3 Shadow Next Instruction Pointer (SNIP). The SNIP is a 32-bit shadow register
8932:that is updated by processor hardware whenever the NIP is updated and shadowing is
8933:enabled. When a precise exception occurs, the SNIP contains a pointer to the next instruc-
8934:tion to be executed. An rte instruction causes instruction prefetch and execution to resume
8935:with the instruction pointed to by the SNIP.
8936:
8937:The SNIP contains two bits that indicate the status of the corresponding instruction and
8938:that can be used to control instruction execution. The valid (V) bit indicates that the SNIP
8939:corresponds to a valid instruction. When the V bit is set, the SNIP contains the address of
8940:the instruction that was being decoded when the exception occurred. When the V bit is
8941:clear, the instruction is invalid. An instruction can be invalid for three reasons. First, if the
8942:instruction pointed to by the SNIP followed a branch or jump instruction, the instruction
8943:is invalid if the .n option was not specified and the branch is taken. Second, the V bit can
8944:be cleared by software, effectively making the instruction corresponding to the NIP stage
8945:a no-operation instruction after execution of an rte instruction and pre-emptying the in-
8946:struction prefetch for that stage. Thirdly, the V bit of SNIP is clear if the V bit of FIP is clear,
8947:the pipeline advances, and shadowing is enabled.
8948:
8949:The exception (E) bit is set when the E bit of the FIP is set, the FIP is updated to NIP, and
8950:shadowing is enabled. This bit can also be set by software with a stcr or xcr instruction.
8951:If the E bit and V bit are set and an rte instruction is executed, the NIP instruction generates
8952:an instruction access exception, regardless of whether the transaction receives a 'success'
8953:or 'fault' reply from the memory system. If the V bit is clear, then the NIP is considered to
8954:be invalid and a no-operation is performed when the XIP is updated with the NIP infor-
8955:mation; the E bit is ignored in this case. Also, when the V bit is clear and an rte instruction
8956:is executed, the instruction corresponding to SNIP is not prefetched. This register has read/
8957:write access.

9278:in 6.4.1 Exception Recognition. When the exception handler software finishes, an rte (that

9281:
9282:The MC88100 includes four trap instructions: the tbnd, tb1, tbO, and tend instruction. The
9283:tbnd instruction is described in 6.5.6 Bounds Check Violation. The tend, tb1, and tbO
9284:instructions can initiate any exception handler by specifying the appropriate vector number
9285:(see Table 6-1). Vectors 0-127 can only be accessed in supervisor mode. The tend, tb1,
9286:and tbO instructions do not begin execution until all scoreboard bits are clear and all data
9287:memory operations are completed. This condition applies regardless of whether or not
9288:the trap is taken.
9289:

9294:registers are modified.

9299:instruction specifies the limit that must be met; if the tested value falls outside of that limit
9300:(out of bounds), the trap is taken. The SXIP points to the tbnd instruction. The bounds test
9301:is an unsigned comparison.

9307:struction that caused the exception. The destination register and carry bit are unchanged
9308:by an instruction that causes an integer overflow exception.

/*
 *  rte:  Return from exception
 */
X(rte)
{
	/*  If executed from user mode, then cause an exception:  */
	if (!(cpu->cd.m88k.cr[M88K_CR_PSR] & M88K_PSR_MODE)) {
		SYNCH_PC;
		m88k_exception(cpu, M88K_EXCEPTION_PRIVILEGE_VIOLATION, 0);
		return;
	}

	m88k_stcr(cpu, cpu->cd.m88k.cr[M88K_CR_EPSR], M88K_CR_PSR, 1);

	if (cpu->cd.m88k.cr[M88K_CR_SNIP] & M88K_NIP_E) {
		debugmsg_cpu(cpu, SUBSYS_CPU, "m88k", VERBOSITY_ERROR,
		    "rte: NIP: TODO: single-step support");
		goto abort_dump;
	}

	if (cpu->cd.m88k.cr[M88K_CR_SFIP] & M88K_FIP_E) {
		debugmsg_cpu(cpu, SUBSYS_CPU, "m88k", VERBOSITY_ERROR,
		    "rte: TODO: FIP single-step support");
		goto abort_dump;
	}

	/*  First try the NIP, if it is Valid:  */
	cpu->pc = cpu->cd.m88k.cr[M88K_CR_SNIP] & M88K_NIP_ADDR;

	/*  If the NIP is not valid, then try the FIP:  */
	if (!(cpu->cd.m88k.cr[M88K_CR_SNIP] & M88K_NIP_V)) {
		/*  Neither the NIP nor the FIP valid?  */
		if (!(cpu->cd.m88k.cr[M88K_CR_SFIP] & M88K_FIP_V)) {
			if ((cpu->cd.m88k.cr[M88K_CR_SFIP] & M88K_FIP_ADDR)
			    != (cpu->cd.m88k.cr[M88K_CR_SNIP] & M88K_NIP_ADDR) + 4) {
				debugmsg_cpu(cpu, SUBSYS_CPU, "m88k", VERBOSITY_ERROR,
		    		    "rte: TODO: Neither FIP nor NIP has the "
				    "Valid bit set?!");
				goto abort_dump;
			}

			/*  For now, continue anyway, using NIP.  */
		} else {
			cpu->pc = cpu->cd.m88k.cr[M88K_CR_SFIP] & M88K_FIP_ADDR;
		}
	} else if (cpu->cd.m88k.cr[M88K_CR_SNIP] & M88K_NIP_V &&
	    cpu->cd.m88k.cr[M88K_CR_SFIP] & M88K_FIP_V &&
	    (cpu->cd.m88k.cr[M88K_CR_SFIP] & M88K_FIP_ADDR)
	    != (cpu->cd.m88k.cr[M88K_CR_SNIP] & M88K_NIP_ADDR) + 4) {
		/*
		 *  The NIP instruction should first be executed (this
		 *  is the one the exception handler choose to return to),
		 *  and then the FIP instruction should run (the target
		 *  of a delayed branch).
		 */

		uint32_t nip = cpu->cd.m88k.cr[M88K_CR_SNIP] & M88K_NIP_ADDR;
		uint32_t fip = cpu->cd.m88k.cr[M88K_CR_SFIP] & M88K_FIP_ADDR;

		cpu->pc = nip;
		cpu->delay_slot = NOT_DELAYED;
		quick_pc_to_pointers(cpu);

		if (cpu->pc != nip) {
			debugmsg_cpu(cpu, SUBSYS_CPU, "m88k", VERBOSITY_ERROR,
			    "NIP execution caused exception?! TODO");
			goto abort_dump;
		}

		instr(to_be_translated)(cpu, cpu->cd.m88k.next_ic);

		if ((cpu->pc & 0xfffff000) != (nip & 0xfffff000)) {
			debugmsg_cpu(cpu, SUBSYS_CPU, "m88k", VERBOSITY_ERROR,
			    "instruction in delay slot when returning via"
			    " rte caused an exception?! nip=0x%08x, but pc "
			    "changed to 0x%08x! TODO", nip, (int)cpu->pc);
			goto abort_dump;
		}

		cpu->pc = fip;
		cpu->delay_slot = NOT_DELAYED;
		quick_pc_to_pointers(cpu);
		return;
	}

	/*  fatal("RTE: NIP=0x%08" PRIx32", FIP=0x%08" PRIx32"\n",
	    cpu->cd.m88k.cr[M88K_CR_SNIP], cpu->cd.m88k.cr[M88K_CR_SFIP]);  */

	quick_pc_to_pointers(cpu);
	return;

abort_dump:
	debugmsg_cpu(cpu, SUBSYS_CPU, "m88k", VERBOSITY_ERROR,
	    "rte failed. NIP=0x%08" PRIx32", FIP=0x%08" PRIx32,
	    cpu->cd.m88k.cr[M88K_CR_SNIP], cpu->cd.m88k.cr[M88K_CR_SFIP]);

	ABORT_EXECUTION;
}


/*
 *  xmem_slow:  Unoptimized xmem (exchange register with memory)
 *
 *  arg[0] = copy of the instruction word
 *
 *  NOTE: The alternative would be to extend the load/store instructions
 *  to also include xmem, and thus implement a "fast" version (where
 *  the page is already accessible both readable and writable) and a
 *  generic version. (The generic implementation would then not need to
 *  have a NO_PC_SYNC flag, since they would always sync.)
 */
X(xmem_slow)
{
	uint32_t iword = ic->arg[0];
	uint32_t tmp;
	int d      = (iword >> 21) & 0x1f;
	int s1     = (iword >> 16) & 0x1f;
	int s2     =  iword        & 0x1f;
	int imm16  =  iword        & 0xffff;
	int regofs = (iword & 0xf0000000) != 0;
	int scaled = iword & 0x200;
	int size   = iword & 0x400;
	int user   = iword & 0x80;
	uint32_t pc_before_memory_access;
	void (*xmem_load)(struct cpu*, struct m88k_instr_call*);
	void (*xmem_store)(struct cpu*, struct m88k_instr_call*);
	struct m88k_instr_call call;

	SYNCH_PC;
	pc_before_memory_access = (uint32_t)cpu->pc;

	if (!regofs) {
		/*  immediate offset:  */
		scaled = 0;
		size = (iword >> 26) & 1;
		user = 0;
	}
 *  else halting.  That is right about the nop and wrong as a fix -- under it
 *  `tcnd eq0,r0,x` still reaches `goto bad`, and 0 == 0 is exactly the case
 *  that MUST trap.  Modelling the condition removes the halt for every form,
 *  and the r0 nop falls out of it for free.
 *
 *  The privilege and vector handling is tb0/tb1's, verbatim: user mode may
 *  not reach the system trap vectors.
 */
X(tcnd)
{
	int32_t v = (int32_t) reg(ic->arg[1]);
	int cls;

	SYNCH_PC;

	if (!(cpu->cd.m88k.cr[M88K_CR_PSR] & M88K_PSR_MODE)
	    && ic->arg[2] < M88K_EXCEPTION_USER_TRAPS_START) {
		m88k_exception(cpu, M88K_EXCEPTION_PRIVILEGE_VIOLATION, 0);
		return;
	}

	if (v == (int32_t) 0x80000000)
		cls = 8;		/*  maximum negative number  */
	else if (v < 0)
		cls = 4;		/*  less than zero  */
	else if (v == 0)
		cls = 2;		/*  equal to zero  */
	else
		cls = 1;		/*  greater than zero  */

	if (ic->arg[0] & cls)
		m88k_exception(cpu, ic->arg[2], 1);
}


/*
 *  tb0, tb1:  Trap on bit Clear/Set
 *
 *  arg[0] = bitmask to check (e.g. 0x00020000 for bit 17)
 *  arg[1] = pointer to register s1
 *  arg[2] = 9-bit vector number
 */
X(tb0)
{
	SYNCH_PC;

	if (!(cpu->cd.m88k.cr[M88K_CR_PSR] & M88K_PSR_MODE)
	    && ic->arg[2] < M88K_EXCEPTION_USER_TRAPS_START) {
		m88k_exception(cpu, M88K_EXCEPTION_PRIVILEGE_VIOLATION, 0);
		return;
	}

	if (!(reg(ic->arg[1]) & ic->arg[0]))
		m88k_exception(cpu, ic->arg[2], 1);
}
X(tb1)
{
	SYNCH_PC;

	if (!(cpu->cd.m88k.cr[M88K_CR_PSR] & M88K_PSR_MODE)
	    && ic->arg[2] < M88K_EXCEPTION_USER_TRAPS_START) {
		m88k_exception(cpu, M88K_EXCEPTION_PRIVILEGE_VIOLATION, 0);
		return;
	}

	if (reg(ic->arg[1]) & ic->arg[0])
		m88k_exception(cpu, ic->arg[2], 1);
}


/*
 *  idle:
 *
 *  s:	ld    rX,rY,ofs

		default:debugmsg_cpu(cpu, SUBSYS_EXCEPTION, "m88k",
			    VERBOSITY_ERROR, "internal error");
			cpu->running = false;
		}
	}

	m88k_pc_to_pointers(cpu);
}

#  BOTH a rung-3 WITNESS (0/9 -> 0/10 -> 2/22 on HEAD 83df95c: every trap-like class stopped
#  the machine; probe10_head.out / probe_v3_head.out) and the DETECTOR (22 fault rows + 2

#  regress/artifacts/m88kexcdeliver/).  Only the detector half runs here.
#
#  The pins are NAMED so that deleting a row is visible: `M88KEXC_RESULT=22/22' pins the
#  denominator (a row that stops running shrinks it); resid50 and sfu1h are the two rows
#  whose CORRECT answer is HALT -- they separate the shipped fix from a form that delivers
#  every vector (the rejected D4) or SFU1 (row m88kexcsfu1); vbrtb is the one ANCHORED row
#  (VBR relocated to 0x2000: it alone reds if the vector base stops being read).

    note "m88k exception-delivery probe produced no completion sentinel; last lines follow"
    tail -5 "$EXCLOG" | sed 's/^/       /'
    check "m88k exc: probe completed" "no" "yes"
else
    check "m88k exc: CR parse self-test (PSR at reset == 0x80000002)" \
          "$(grep -c 'M88KEXC_SELFTEST=OK' "$EXCLOG")" "1"
    check "m88k exc: 22 fault rows, all as expected (the denominator is the pin)" \
          "$(grep -c 'M88KEXC_RESULT=22/22' "$EXCLOG")" "1"
    check "m88k exc: the two liveness/attribution controls computed their quotients" \
          "$(grep -cE '^M88KEXC_ROW=(live|attrib) +RESULT=DONE' "$EXCLOG")" "2"
    check "m88k exc: a residual vector (50) still HALTS at the fallback (negative control vs D4)" \
          "$(grep -cE '^M88KEXC_ROW=resid50 +RESULT=HALT' "$EXCLOG")" "1"
    check "m88k exc: SFU1 inside a handler still HALTS (the m88kexcsfu1 boundary)" \
          "$(grep -cE '^M88KEXC_ROW=sfu1h +RESULT=HALT' "$EXCLOG")" "1"

          "$(grep -c 'bad=.*level' "$EXCLOG")" "0"

8888:r31 r30 r29 r28 r27 r26 r25 r24 r23 r22 r21 r20 r19 r18 r17 r16 r15 r14 r13 r12 r11 r10 r9 r8 r7 r6 r5 r4 r3 r2 r1 rO

8890:          SSBR                                                                   cr3
8891:
8892:                                                     NOTE
8893:
8894:After an MC88100 reset, the contents of SSBR are not defined. The SSBR must
8895:be explicitly cleared before shadowing is enabled. This guarantees that the SSBR
8896:reflects the SB when shadowing is enabled.
8897:
8898:6.4.3.1.2 Shadow Fetch Instruction Pointer (SFIP). The SFIP is a 32-bit register that shad-
8899:ows the FIP when shadowing is enabled. When a precise exception occurs, the SFIP contains
8900:a pointer either to the instruction following the next instruction or to the target of a jump
8901:or branch instruction. An rte instruction fetches the instruction pointed to by the SNIP and
8902:then the instruction pointed to by the SFIP.
8903:
8904:The SFIP contains a valid (V) and an exception (E) bit. The V bit can be cleared by software,
8905:effectively making the corresponding instruction a no-operation instruction when an rte is
8906:executed. In this case, the corresponding instruction is not prefetched from memory. If
8907:both the V and E bits are set, an rte instruction causes the instruction pointed to by the
8908:SFIP to generate an instruction access exception when it advances to the NIP, regardless
8909:of whether the transaction receives a 'success' or 'fault' reply from the memory system.
8910:An rte instruction can be made to return to a particular instruction by placing a valid
8911:instruction address in the SNIP and the next sequential instruction address in the SFIP
8912:(with V bits set and E bits clear). The rte resumes execution at the instruction pointed to
8913:by the SNIP, then the SFIP. This register has read/write access.
8914:
8915:31                                                                                    I V  E
8916:                                                                  SHADOW OF FIP
8917:
8918:          SFIP                                                                   cr6
8919:
8920:Bits\31-2 - Shadow of Fetch Instruction Pointer
8921:
8922:V-Valid
8923:      0 - Fetch instruction is not valid (corresponds to no-operation); ignore E bit.
8924:      1 - Fetch instruction is valid.
8925:
8926:E - Exception
8927:      0 - Fetch instruction is fetched normally.
8928:      1 - Force instruction access fault if execution of the instruction is attempted.
8929:
8930:MOTOROLA  MC88100 USER'S MANUAL                                                            6-11
8931:6.4.3.1.3 Shadow Next Instruction Pointer (SNIP). The SNIP is a 32-bit shadow register
8932:that is updated by processor hardware whenever the NIP is updated and shadowing is
8933:enabled. When a precise exception occurs, the SNIP contains a pointer to the next instruc-
8934:tion to be executed. An rte instruction causes instruction prefetch and execution to resume
8935:with the instruction pointed to by the SNIP.
8936:
8937:The SNIP contains two bits that indicate the status of the corresponding instruction and
8938:that can be used to control instruction execution. The valid (V) bit indicates that the SNIP
8939:corresponds to a valid instruction. When the V bit is set, the SNIP contains the address of
8940:the instruction that was being decoded when the exception occurred. When the V bit is
8941:clear, the instruction is invalid. An instruction can be invalid for three reasons. First, if the
8942:instruction pointed to by the SNIP followed a branch or jump instruction, the instruction
8943:is invalid if the .n option was not specified and the branch is taken. Second, the V bit can
8944:be cleared by software, effectively making the instruction corresponding to the NIP stage
8945:a no-operation instruction after execution of an rte instruction and pre-emptying the in-
8946:struction prefetch for that stage. Thirdly, the V bit of SNIP is clear if the V bit of FIP is clear,
8947:the pipeline advances, and shadowing is enabled.
8948:
8949:The exception (E) bit is set when the E bit of the FIP is set, the FIP is updated to NIP, and
8950:shadowing is enabled. This bit can also be set by software with a stcr or xcr instruction.
8951:If the E bit and V bit are set and an rte instruction is executed, the NIP instruction generates
8952:an instruction access exception, regardless of whether the transaction receives a 'success'
8953:or 'fault' reply from the memory system. If the V bit is clear, then the NIP is considered to
8954:be invalid and a no-operation is performed when the XIP is updated with the NIP infor-
8955:mation; the E bit is ignored in this case. Also, when the V bit is clear and an rte instruction
8956:is executed, the instruction corresponding to SNIP is not prefetched. This register has read/
8957:write access.
8958:
8959:31                                                                                 IV E
8960:                                                               SHADOW OF NIP
8961:
8962:      SNIP                                                                    cr5
8963:

GXEMUL-SEC/regress/artifacts/m88kexcdeliver/opus_p2.md-34-**(3) M_SFIPNOTGT / M_DSLOTSWAP — L3, a channel vacuous by construction.** This is not a missing cell; the `dslot` cell is rowed. It is the pre-table CHANNEL check's own failure mode, and it is arithmetic visible in the round's *own* BASE output: the row wants `snip=(fault_pc+4)|V` and `sfip=(CODE+4+8)|V`, written as two independent expectations that both evaluate to **0x1000e**. `br.n +8` at 0x10004 puts `delay_target` at 0x1000c and the slot at 0x10008, so `delay_target == slot_pc + 4` exactly. The two channels are one number, so the delay-slot arm's SFIP can stop reading `delay_target` altogether, or trade places with SNIP, and the row cannot vary. The round's own M_DSLOT kill (8/9, `sfip=0x10012`) proves only that *a numerically different* SFIP is caught — not that the `delay_target` channel is pinned. The `dslot` row is the sole carrier of the delay-slot arm's obligation, and half of it is vacuous.
GXEMUL-SEC/regress/artifacts/m88kexcdeliver/opus_p2.md-35-
GXEMUL-SEC/regress/artifacts/m88kexcdeliver/opus_p2.md:36:**(4) M_DELAYALWAYS and M_TAILOFF — clause-(e) coverage debt, disclose and file.** Both leave the fix's own repair intact (the seven still deliver), so neither is a floor breach. M_DELAYALWAYS shows the `else` branch of the post-delivery `cpu->delay_slot` write is unpinned: the `if` branch *is* pinned, because `X(br_n)` (`cpu_m88k_instr.c:99-105`) overwrites `cpu->pc` with `delay_target` unless `EXCEPTION_IN_DELAY_SLOT` is set — that is what makes the `dslot` row work. The `else` branch needs a second **unfrozen** exception to show damage, and the only two-fault row (`error`) freezes. M_TAILOFF escapes because the probe has **no row for vectors 0..3**, whose tail arms do real work (`SNIP -= 4` for INTERRUPT, `SNIP/SFIP = 0` for INSTRUCTION_ACCESS, the DMT copy for DATA_ACCESS).
GXEMUL-SEC/regress/artifacts/m88kexcdeliver/opus_p2.md-37-
GXEMUL-SEC/regress/artifacts/m88kexcdeliver/opus_p2.md-38-**M_ISTRAPBOUNDS is a result about the code, not an escape.** I enumerated every raise site (`grep -rn 'm88k_exception('` over `src/`): BOUNDS_CHECK_VIOLATION has **no literal-constant site at all** — `tb0`/`tb1`/`tcnd` at `cpu_m88k_instr.c:2529/2551/2564` are the only route and all three pass `is_trap = 1`. So `implemented = is_trap` on that arm is equivalent in today's tree; the mutant cannot die because the target is dead. Same for the ERROR arm (switch 1's ERROR case is reachable only by a trap naming 10 — which answers the brief's own open QUESTION at the end of §3: **no, there is no other route**, and the SFRZ re-route provably cannot reach it because it runs after switch 1). It becomes non-equivalent the day `m88ktbnd` lands, since `tbnd` raises bounds-check (UM `:9298`).

GXEMUL-SEC/regress/artifacts/m88kexcdeliver/opus_p2.md-52-**Q2 — the smallest edit that breaks the diff and still passes.** `cpu->pc = 8 * vector;` — a **31-byte deletion**, one expression, 10/10. GXEMUL-SEC/regress/artifacts/m88kexcdeliver/opus_p2.md-53-
GXEMUL-SEC/regress/artifacts/m88kexcdeliver/opus_p2.md:54:**Q3 — which of the sixteen dispositions is wrong.** I think **F14 is the one that is wrong**, and it is wrong in a way the round can check in two minutes: *"the carry bit is a second unasserted destination -> the exact PSR compare pins bit 28; ACCEPTED as covered."* The exact PSR compare pins bit 28 **to zero**, and zero is the pre-fault value in every row, so it covers the direction that cannot fail and leaves the direction the manual actually asserts ("unchanged") unobservable. glm's finding was live and was closed on a half-argument. Two others I would sharpen rather than overturn: **F3's stated warrant proves too much** — "both reached today" admits BOUNDS by the fact that a supervisor trap can name any vector, and that same argument admits all 119 residual vectors, which the round then declined to adopt as D4; the ruling is right but its load-bearing reason is the *architectural-category* one (the SH precedent), not reachability. And **F15's citation is weaker than the evidence available** — it cites Figure 6-4, while §6.10 at `:10613-10617` states it in prose: *"The error exception occurs when shadowing is frozen … and an exception other than a trap instruction occurs. When shadowing is frozen, the shadow registers do not contain valid data."* That prose is also the warrant for repair 2 below. Every other disposition I checked held.
GXEMUL-SEC/regress/artifacts/m88kexcdeliver/opus_p2.md-55-
GXEMUL-SEC/regress/artifacts/m88kexcdeliver/opus_p2.md-56-**Q4 — what all of us missed.** Three things, all in one family: **the panel checked whether each register's VALUE is asserted and never whether its DERIVATION is pinned.** F1 opened exactly the right question ("printed but not asserted") and it was closed at the value level. `pc` is asserted as a number reachable without reading VBR; `SNIP` and `SFIP` are asserted as two numbers that are the same number; the carry bit is asserted at the one value that cannot discriminate. All three are knowable at row-writing time by arithmetic on the row's own constants — no table needed — which is precisely what the CHANNEL line in the pre-table check is for, and `PRETABLE_m88kexc.out` was written before table 1. A fourth, smaller: nobody rowed vectors 0..3, so the comment's own comparison ("like the four this fallback never covered") rests on four classes the detector never exercises.

web search: https://www.bitsavers.org/components/motorola/88000/MC88100_RISC_Microprocessor_Users_Manual_2ed_1990.pdf
codex
Two accepted dispositions conflict with the code. `M_TAILOFF` uses `< 4`, so it preserves all four hardware-event arms; their absence from the probe does not explain its survival. Also, eleven low vectors pass the fallback, but RESET still stops CPUs in the tail arm, making “eleven delivered” incorrect.
