void arm_exception(struct cpu *cpu, int exception_nr)
{
	int oldmode, newmode;
	uint32_t retaddr;

	if (exception_nr < 0 || exception_nr >= N_ARM_EXCEPTIONS) {
		fatal("arm_exception(): exception_nr = %i\n", exception_nr);
		exit(1);
	}

	retaddr = cpu->pc;

	if (!quiet_mode) {
		debug("[ arm_exception(): ");
		switch (exception_nr) {
		case ARM_EXCEPTION_RESET:
			fatal("RESET: TODO");
			break;
		case ARM_EXCEPTION_UND:
			debug("UNDEFINED");
			break;
		case ARM_EXCEPTION_SWI:
			debug("SWI");
			break;
		case ARM_EXCEPTION_PREF_ABT:
			debug("PREFETCH ABORT");
			break;
		case ARM_EXCEPTION_IRQ:
			debug("IRQ");
			break;
		case ARM_EXCEPTION_FIQ:
			debug("FIQ");
			break;
		case ARM_EXCEPTION_DATA_ABT:
			debug("DATA ABORT, far=0x%08x fsr=0x%02x",
			    cpu->cd.arm.far, cpu->cd.arm.fsr);
			break;
		}
		debug(" ]\n");
	}

	switch (exception_nr) {
	case ARM_EXCEPTION_RESET:
		cpu->running = 0;
		fatal("ARM RESET: TODO");
		exit(1);
	case ARM_EXCEPTION_DATA_ABT:
		retaddr += 4;
		break;
	}

	retaddr += (cpu->cd.arm.cpsr & ARM_FLAG_T ? 2 : 4);

	arm_save_register_bank(cpu);

	cpu->cd.arm.cpsr &= 0x0fffffff;
	cpu->cd.arm.cpsr |= (cpu->cd.arm.flags << 28);

	switch (arm_exception_to_mode[exception_nr]) {
	case ARM_MODE_SVC32:
		cpu->cd.arm.spsr_svc = cpu->cd.arm.cpsr; break;
	case ARM_MODE_ABT32:
		cpu->cd.arm.spsr_abt = cpu->cd.arm.cpsr; break;
	case ARM_MODE_UND32:
		cpu->cd.arm.spsr_und = cpu->cd.arm.cpsr; break;
	case ARM_MODE_IRQ32:
		cpu->cd.arm.spsr_irq = cpu->cd.arm.cpsr; break;
	case ARM_MODE_FIQ32:
		cpu->cd.arm.spsr_fiq = cpu->cd.arm.cpsr; break;
	default:fatal("arm_exception(): unimplemented exception nr\n");
		exit(1);
	}

	/*
	 *  Disable Thumb mode (because exception handlers always execute
	 *  in ARM mode), set the exception mode, and disable interrupts:
	 */
	cpu->cd.arm.cpsr &= ~ARM_FLAG_T;

	oldmode = cpu->cd.arm.cpsr & ARM_FLAG_MODE;

	cpu->cd.arm.cpsr &= ~ARM_FLAG_MODE;
	cpu->cd.arm.cpsr |= arm_exception_to_mode[exception_nr];

	/*
	 *  Usually, an exception should change modes (so that saved status
	 *  bits don't get lost). However, Linux on ARM seems to use floating
	 *  point instructions in the kernel (!), and it emulates those using
	 *  its own fp emulation code. This leads to a situation where we
	 *  sometimes change from SVC32 to SVC32.
	 */
	newmode = cpu->cd.arm.cpsr & ARM_FLAG_MODE;
	if (oldmode == newmode && oldmode != ARM_MODE_SVC32) {
		fatal("[ WARNING! Exception caused no mode change? "
		    "mode 0x%02x (pc=0x%x) ]\n", newmode, (int)cpu->pc);
		/*  exit(1);  */
	}

	cpu->cd.arm.cpsr |= ARM_FLAG_I;
	if (exception_nr == ARM_EXCEPTION_RESET ||
	    exception_nr == ARM_EXCEPTION_FIQ)
		cpu->cd.arm.cpsr |= ARM_FLAG_F;

	/*  Load the new register bank, if we switched:  */
	arm_load_register_bank(cpu);

	/*
	 *  Set the return address and new PC.
	 *
	 *  NOTE: r[ARM_PC] is also set; see cpu_arm_instr_loadstore.c for
	 *  details. (If an exception occurs during a load into the pc
	 *  register, the code in that file assumes that the r[ARM_PC]
	 *  was changed to the address of the exception handler.)
	 */
	cpu->cd.arm.r[ARM_LR] = retaddr;
	cpu->pc = cpu->cd.arm.r[ARM_PC] = exception_nr * 4 +
	    ((cpu->cd.arm.control & ARM_CONTROL_V)? 0xffff0000 : 0);
	quick_pc_to_pointers(cpu);
}


/*
 *  arm_cpu_tlbdump():
 *
 *  Called from the debugger to dump the TLB in a readable format.
 *
		 */
		cpu->is_halted = false;
		cpu->pc += sizeof(uint16_t);
	}

	if (cpu->delay_slot) {
		cpu->delay_slot = EXCEPTION_IN_DELAY_SLOT;
		cpu->pc -= sizeof(uint16_t);
	}


	/*
	 *  Stuff common to all exceptions:
	 */

	cpu->cd.sh.spc = cpu->pc;
	cpu->cd.sh.ssr = cpu->cd.sh.sr;
	cpu->cd.sh.sgr = cpu->cd.sh.r[15];

	if (intevt > 0) {
		cpu->cd.sh.intevt = intevt;
		expevt = -1;
	} else {
		cpu->cd.sh.expevt = expevt;
	}

	sh_update_sr(cpu, cpu->cd.sh.sr | SH_SR_MD | SH_SR_RB | SH_SR_BL);

	/*  Most exceptions set PC to VBR + 0x100.  */
	cpu->pc = vbr + 0x100;


	/*  Specific cases:  */
	switch (expevt) {

	case -1:	/*  Interrupt  */
		cpu->pc = vbr + 0x600;
		break;

	/*
	 *  #457: EXPEVT_RESET_TLB_MULTI_HIT joins this group, and joining it is
	 *  the whole fix.  dev_sh4.c raises it when a guest ASSOCIATIVE write to
	 *  the UTLB address array matches more than one entry; this switch had no
	 *  arm for it, so it fell to the default below and ran exit(1).  Three
	 *  ordinary privileged guest stores ended the host process.
	 *
	 *  The manual puts it in exactly this group.  **Table 5.2 Exceptions**
	 *  (_scratchpad/sh7750_hardware.txt:11994, rows at :12006 and :12010) lists
	 *  BOTH TLB multiple-hit exceptions as Reset / Abort category, vector
	 *  H'A000 0000, code H'140 -- the same rows as power-on and manual reset.
	 *
	 *  The arm below is NOT Manual_reset() transcribed.  That routine (:12392)
	 *  is ten statements and includes Initialize_CPU() and
	 *  Initialize_Module(Manual); this arm is three, because EXPEVT and
	 *  MD|RB|BL are already done by the common code above, and because NOT
	 *  reinitialising the CPU is the faithful part here -- 3.6.4's software
	 *  processing inspects the UTLB entries AFTER the reset, which a full
	 *  re-init would destroy.
	 *
	 *  EXPEVT survives the grouping by construction: the common code above
	 *  assigns cpu->cd.sh.expevt BEFORE this switch, so 0x140 is already
	 *  latched and the guest's reset handler can tell a multiple hit from a
	 *  power-on (0x000) or a manual reset (0x020).  A review seat asked
	 *  whether grouping would clobber it; it does not.
	 *
	 *  *** DO NOT REWRITE THIS AS A SEPARATE ARM THAT SETS pc AND vbr ONLY. ***
	 *  That is the likelier implementation than the fix itself, and it was
	 *  MEASURED passing a witness that pins alive + expevt + pc + vbr -- because
	 *  sh_update_sr() is a NO-OP from the reset-default SR (IMASK already 0xf,
	 *  FD already clear).  It only becomes observable when the guest holds FD or
	 *  a low IMASK at fault time, which is why the detector pre-sets SR and pins
	 *  the result.  Figure 5.2 (:12228) specifies SR.{MD,RB,BL,FD,IMASK} <-
	 *  11101111 for the reset transition, so the third statement is required,
	 *  not decoration.
	 *
	 *  Deleting the detection instead was considered and is REFUTED by
	 *  **3.7.4 UTLB Address Array** (:10111, sentence at :10141-10143), which
	 *  both raises the exception AND supplies the ordering: "D and V specified
	 *  in the data field are written to that entry.  If there is more than one
	 *  matching entry, a data TLB multiple hit exception results."  Write first,
	 *  then raise -- so dev_sh4.c's update-before-count is faithful and a
	 *  scan-first rewrite would be less so.
	 *
	 *  (An earlier draft cited 3.6.4's "the contents of PPN ... may also be
	 *  corrupted" as that warrant.  A review seat pointed out it is the wrong
	 *  sentence: the device loop writes V and D, never PPN.  The conclusion was
	 *  right and the reason was not, which is the kind of thing that gets
	 *  reused.)
	 */
	case EXPEVT_RESET_POWER:
	case EXPEVT_RESET_MANUAL:
	case EXPEVT_RESET_TLB_MULTI_HIT:
		cpu->pc = 0xa0000000;
		cpu->cd.sh.vbr = 0x00000000;
		sh_update_sr(cpu, (cpu->cd.sh.sr | SH_SR_IMASK) & ~SH_SR_FD);
		break;

	case EXPEVT_TLB_MISS_LD:
	case EXPEVT_TLB_MISS_ST:
		cpu->pc = vbr + 0x400;
		// fall through
	case EXPEVT_TLB_PROT_LD:
	case EXPEVT_TLB_PROT_ST:
	case EXPEVT_TLB_MOD:
		cpu->cd.sh.tea = vaddr;
		cpu->cd.sh.pteh &= ~SH4_PTEH_VPN_MASK;
		cpu->cd.sh.pteh |= (vaddr & SH4_PTEH_VPN_MASK);
		break;

	case EXPEVT_TRAPA:
		/*
		 *  Note: The TRA register is already set by the implementation
		 *  of the trapa instruction. See cpu_sh_instr.c for details.
		 *  Here, spc is incremented, so that a return from the trap
		 *  handler transfers control to the instruction _following_
		 *  the trapa.
		 */
		cpu->cd.sh.spc += sizeof(uint16_t);
		break;

	case EXPEVT_RES_INST:
		/*
		 *  Note: Having this code here makes it possible to catch
		 *  reserved instructions; during normal instruction execution,
		 *  these are not very common.
		 */
		/*  #217: (Codex/Fable) a guest reserved instruction must take the
		    illegal-instruction exception (the general vector is set up
		    above), not exit() the host.  */
		break;

		 *  putting a trapa or an FP instruction in a branch delay slot

The negative controls also have a false-pass path: the classifier labels any matching `unimplemented … vector=` message as `HALT` without requiring the machine to have stopped or checking the expected vector. The recorded `M_REVERT` run confirms that `sfu1h` passes even when execution stops at the initial divide, before reaching the SFU1 handler.

799-The following paragraphs provide a brief overview of the architecture of the MC88100.
800-Topics such as register-to-register operations, addressing modes, instruction formats, de-
801:layed branching, privileged levels, memory ports, and optimizing software are discussed.
802-
803-1.2.1 Register-to-Register Architecture
--
845-1.2.5 Delayed Branching
846-
847:The MC88100 incorporates delayed branching to reduce pipeline penalties associated with
848-changes in program flow. In processors incorporating pipelined execution, changes in the
849-program flow can reduce execution speed due to the time required to flush and refill the
850:pipeline. However, the MC88100 takes advantage of delayed branching, which allows the
851:instruction fetched after the branch instruction to be optionally executed, whether or not
852:the branch is taken. Consequently, the pipeline continues to operate without unused cycles.
853:The next instruction in the pipeline executes while the branch target instruction is pre-
854-fetched from memory.
855-
856:The execution of the instruction following the branch is under explicit software control
857:through the value of a bit in the instruction encoding. When delayed branching is selected,
858-the programmer or compiler picks a useful instruction to be executed before the change
859:of flow and places the instruction after the branch in the instruction stream.
860-
861-1.2.6 Levels of Privileges
--
899-performance. This software performs optimizations based on the concurrent execution
900-pipelines; instructions are scheduled to avoid pipeline stalls due to data dependencies.
901:Delayed branches are used a high percentage ofthe time. This software also makes efficient
902-use of the MC88100 instruction set and register model.
903-
--

1015:ditionalbranch instructions with displacement, and exception vectoring. Other tasks, such
1016-as partial instruction decoding and tasks related to subroutine returns and exception proc-
1017-essing, are also performed. Registers are maintained that indicate the contents of the

1031-The instruction unit identifies and saves the return pointer for jsr and bsr instructions to

1033:return pointer is either the contents of the NIP or FIP at the time the jsr or bsr instruction
1034:begins execution, depending on whether or not delayed branching is used.

codex
The delay-slot dispute comes from confusing two pipeline stages: when the slot executes, the branch target is already the next instruction. The label can honestly preserve a known behavior, but the row cannot certify architectural correctness. The arithmetic checks also show that Table 1 has eleven killed mutants and two survivors, totaling thirteen.
codex

- **F2 — UPHOLD:** `privsuptb` supplies `is_trap=1`; `privtb` reaches PRIVILEGE through the user-mode filter with zero. Anchors: `privsuptb`, `X(tb1)`.

- **F12 — OVERTURN:** `< MISALIGNED`, meaning `< 4`, preserves the hardware-event arms. Their absence cannot explain this mutant’s survival. Anchor: [`Vector-specific handling`](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/cpus/cpu_m88k.c:881).

- **D1 — REFINE:** The dissent snapshots the earlier branch stage. While the slot executes, NIP is the target and FIP is target+4; an emulator-only return test cannot independently establish that architectural oracle. Anchors: §6.4.3.1.2/.3 in the [MC88100 manual](https://archive.decromancer.ca/bitsavers.org/components/motorola/88000/MC88100_RISC_Microprocessor_Users_Manual_2ed_1990.pdf).
- **D2 — OVERTURN:** “Both true” is incorrect for the specified mutant: both guards admit vectors 0–3. `M_TAILOFF` is equivalent throughout the current reachable tail behavior.
- **D3 — UPHOLD:** Observability and the INFO choice are supported by filtering and breakpoint behavior; the narrower detector limitation is F7. Anchor: `debugmsg_break_on_error`.

**Q2. The clear incorrect acceptances are F12/D2 and F20.** The census correction attributed to Codex was itself wrong.

For `M_TAILOFF`, enumeration gives:

| Vector values | Effect of changing `< 128` to `< 4` |
|---|---|
| `0,1,2,3` | Both execute the same active arm |
| `4,5,6,7,8,9,10` | Executing a bare break becomes skipping the switch |
| `11..127` | Both return at the earlier fallback |
| `128..511` | Both skip the tail |

The SFRZ reroute precedes this comparison. I also enumerated all 512 vector values × four SFRZ/trap combinations: **2,048 combinations, zero differences in tail actions**. That is a static equivalence check, not an emulator measurement. Missing hardware-event rows remain separate coverage debt; they cannot kill this particular mutant.

For F20, with SFRZ initially clear:

| Population | Count | Outcome |
|---|---:|---|
| `0` | 1 | RESET tail stops CPUs |
| `1,2,3` | 3 | Existing delivery paths |
| `4,5,6,7,8,9,10` | 7 | Added delivery paths |
| `11..113` | 103 | Fallback stops CPUs |
| `114,115` | 2 | Fallback stops CPUs |
| `116..127` | 12 | Fallback stops CPUs |

Thus **11 bypass the fallback; 10 continue through delivery; 117 stop at the fallback; 118 stop overall**. This counts control flow, not architectural correctness of the hardware-vector trap cases.

F9’s acceptance needs a detector repair, and F15’s comment repair still needs the freeze qualification.

**Q3. The shared packet misses two concrete detector gaps and overstates another channel.**

1. **IND never starts clear.** The pre-exception PSRs are `0x80000002`, `0x00000002`, `0x90000002`, or the nested-handler value `0x8000000b`. Every one has IND set. Omitting `| M88K_PSR_IND` therefore produces the same compared values. This is the same mistake previously made about MODE and carry. An existing suitable row can start with IND clear and assert `EPSR.IND=0`, `PSR.IND=1`; measure the corresponding omission mutant. Anchors: `PSR_RESET`, `PSR_USER`, `PSR_CARRY`, `disable interrupts`.

2. **HALT is presently a diagnostic classification, not a verified stopped state.** The [classifier](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/m88k_exc_probe.py:285) computes `stopped` but accepts `halted` first without consulting it. Consequently, a diagnostic-preserving omission of the stop operation is a predicted survivor. Moreover, any vector diagnostic qualifies: [`M_REVERT.out`](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/artifacts/m88kexcdeliver/killtable3/M_REVERT.out:23), anchor `M88KEXC_ROW=sfu1h`, passes at `pc=0x10004` with untouched shadows—the initial division stopped execution before the handler ran. Require the stopped-state observation and expected vector; for `sfu1h`, also establish that the second stage was reached.

3. **The level channel proves absence at WARNING.** INFO and DEBUG are both silent there. Either narrow the contract to detecting the ERROR/WARNING regression, or add an INFO-visibility observation if exact INFO is the promised property. Anchors: `VERBOSITY_INFO`, `VERBOSITY_DEBUG`, `debugmsg_add_verbosity_level(SUBSYS_ALL, -1)`.

There are also record corrections. Enumerating Table 1’s thirteen `M_*.out` summaries gives **eleven killed and two survivors**, `M_LEVEL` and `M_ERRORDROP`. The gate comment’s `2/22` also conflicts with the recorded reversal’s `3/22`. `PRETABLE_m88kexc.out` still contains the old unobservable-LEVEL and “SFIP wrong” descriptions; its claimed corrections need an explicit annotation.

For D1, the stage sequence is:

`branch executing: (XIP, NIP, FIP) = (branch, slot, target)`  
`slot executing: (XIP, NIP, FIP) = (slot, target, target+4)`

The latter is the relevant precise-fault snapshot. Keeping the current dslot pin with its warning is honest; calling its passing result “shadow registers right” or proof of correct return addresses is not. An emulator return trace verifies implementation consequences, while the manual supplies the architectural expectation.

**My denominator:** I inspected all 24 program rows—20 expected deliveries, two expected halts, two arithmetic controls—plus the reset parser check; all twelve reachable switch-1 class/route cells; all four SFRZ/trap cells; the five named PSR obligations; EPSR, shadow, VBR, tail, and logging assertions; and all fourteen supplied v3 mutant shapes. Their recorded outcomes are thirteen rejected and one equivalent. The route additions, VBR omission, frozen-trap reroute, carry-clear case, vector-128 bound, and named shadow permutations have discriminating observations. The dslot observations remain implementation pins.

**This denominator does not close:** IND transition coverage and HALT verification fail it. Exact INFO remains unpinned if retained as the contract. The disclosed delay-state, hardware-event, DMT, SSBR, and FP-register debts remain outside that claim.
